statement can distinguish them. This is the intended Ferret Frenzy texture: uncertainty constrained
by rules rather than randomness without structure.


PART IV — SYSTEM CONTRACTS, EDGE CASES, AND IMPLEMENTATION RECONCILIATION
=======================================================================

Purpose of this pass. Revision 3.1 is an implementation-reconciliation pass over Revision 3.0. It does not add a ninth role, a third die, a new faction, a new win condition, or a new presentation layer. The eight-role library, fixed twenty-four-card pool, player-count Active Set recipes, three Hammock cards, d12 Burrow Hours, d6 special checks, historical Raider objective, Morning Business, simultaneous Paw Point, and player-bound conversion/history model remain intact. The purpose of this section is to define the places where a correct game can still drift if two individually reasonable implementations make different assumptions. Where this section conflicts with an earlier ambiguous sentence, this section controls. Where it merely restates an earlier rule more precisely, the earlier role chapter remains valid.

The inspected Revision 3 frontend package already implements the central Revision 3 ideas: role-neutral lobby seats, backend-authoritative random deal, separate Starting Role and Current Card concepts, timestamp-aware claims, Business single-result inspection normalization, Dooker stale Glimpse reasoning, Trouble conversion before swap, Snuggle Bond as reveal rather than extra vote capture, Guardian protection as capture-eligibility removal, chat that remains open through voting, and specialized bots that are intended to reason only from authorized facts. Its automated package tests pass. Revision 3.1 therefore treats those behaviors as the current implementation baseline while making the rules contract stricter than any one UI helper. Frontend convenience is never the authority for hidden-state legality.


1. Authority ladder and disagreement handling
--------------------------------------------

Ferret Frenzy now uses an explicit authority ladder so future passes do not accidentally let a convenience layer redefine the game. First is the gameplay dossier: it defines intended legal states, legal actions, information rights, event order, and result semantics. Second is the authoritative game state and backend resolution: it must implement those rules and must reject illegal client requests. Third are compatibility normalizers: they may narrow legacy payloads into the dossier shape, but they may not create facts that did not exist. Fourth are frontend presentation, Quick Role Claim, bot heuristics, and replay summaries: these may explain or reason about state, but they do not make hidden claims true and do not grant actions.

If the frontend says a target is selectable but the backend contract says it is illegal, the backend must reject it. If a bot heuristic scores a player as suspicious but the dossier says the bot lacks the underlying fact, the score must not use that fact. If a compatibility layer receives a legacy array where Revision 3 permits only one Business inspection result, it may deterministically choose one authorized result for presentation, but it may not reveal the rest. If post-game replay exposes a hidden event, that does not imply the event was visible during the round.

The game should record disagreements as implementation defects rather than silently changing the rulebook. A future code pass may intentionally change a rule, but that change must be written into a new dossier revision and propagated to the help text, bot doctrine, tests, and result semantics together.


2. Canonical state axes: never collapse these fields
---------------------------------------------------

Every participant can be described along several separate axes. Starting Role is the immutable dealt role and is the only role that schedules night actions. Current Card is the card presently occupying the player's position and can move through Dooker Trip or Trouble swap. Current Allegiance is derived at a timestamp using the precedence rules. Historical Raider is a player-bound historical status. Trouble In On It and Hunter conversion are player-bound statuses. Snuggle Bond is a player relationship. Hunt Mark is an event-bound target relation created by loyal Hunter. Guardian protection is a one-resolution eligibility modifier. Caught is a result state produced by Paw Point or valid Hunt Mark, not by ordinary reveal.

These fields must remain independent even when several happen to agree. A participant can start Bandit, lose the Bandit Current Card, become Business-side under the ordinary Current Card rule, remember the original Bandit roster, and still not be the historical Raider. A participant can start Business, receive a Bandit Current Card, become Bandit-side without becoming a starting Bandit and without learning the historical Raider. A converted Hunter can later hold a Business Current Card and still remain Bandit-side because conversion outranks Current Card. Historical Raider remains Bandit-side even if every card-movement effect removes the Bandit card from that seat.

No UI label such as TEAM, ROLE, CARD, or SIDE may substitute for all of these concepts at once. Replays and debug tools should display the axes separately. Bots should store them separately. Quick Role Claim should continue to distinguish a Starting Role claim from a Current Card claim. A statement that a player started Guardian can be historically true at the same time that a statement about that player's final card is different.


3. What players know about their own Current Card and allegiance
---------------------------------------------------------------

Card movement is intentionally capable of creating uncertainty. Therefore a player does not automatically learn that their Current Card changed merely because Dooker or Trouble moved a card. A moved Current Card is not privately re-revealed to its new holder during the night or Morning Business unless a specific rule grants that information. Final Current Cards are revealed in Results. This preserves the one-night-style distinction between what a player started as and what card may now occupy the seat.

The same fairness rule applies to current-card-derived allegiance. The server may compute Current Allegiance internally at every timestamp because Dooker Glimpse and final resolution need it, but a participant is not automatically entitled to an otherwise hidden Current Card or derived allegiance simply because the server knows it. A player does know player-bound status changes that happen to them when the role explicitly grants that knowledge: historical Raider knows they are Raider; Trouble who becomes In On It knows the conversion and Raider identity; Hunter knows the private conversion roll and whether it converted; a starting Bandit knows the starting Bandit roster and Raider identity. Those are explicit private facts, not deductions from a hidden Current Card.

This rule is especially important for bot parity. A bot may receive a transport object that contains internal fields for resolution or compatibility, but its decision layer must not use a hidden Current Card or derived allegiance unless the equivalent human player is entitled to know it. The safest implementation is to provide bots with an information-filtered player view identical in substance to the human private-information panel, then add only public chat and public result data. Any bot helper that treats a raw `me.allegiance` field as automatically player-known must first establish that the allegiance was authorized by an explicit fact or by an intentionally public reveal.


4. Role-card reveal and pre-night readiness
-------------------------------------------

Each occupied seat receives one Starting Card from the authoritative shuffled Active Set. The player must be allowed to review that private Starting Role before any role-specific pre-night choice or required die roll whose meaning depends on that role. Snuggler's bond, Guardian-Hunter recognition, Itchy's activation check, multi-wake d12 schedule, and Bandit tiebreak setup all belong after the Starting Role has been privately delivered.

Acknowledging the role card is a presentation gate, not a new game mechanic. It prevents a player from accidentally taking a pre-night action before reading the role. Reconnect may re-present the player's own Starting Role if needed because that role is already authorized private information. Reconnect must not reveal a different player's role, Hammock card, raw die roll, or action prompt.

Lobby seats remain role-neutral. A human or bot joining a seat cannot reserve, preview, request, or bias a role. Bots are ordinary seats before the deal and load the specialized role brain only after their own Starting Role is authoritatively assigned.


5. Active Set recipes and Hammock invariants
--------------------------------------------

For four through ten players, the Active Set contains exactly player count plus three cards. Three cards always remain in the Hammock after one Starting Card is dealt to each occupied seat. The recipe describes the cards available to be shuffled, not the roles guaranteed to be player-held. A unique role can be in the recipe and still sit in the Hammock. No client should infer that a role must be held because it appears in the player-count recipe.

The fixed global pool remains four Bandit, fourteen Business, and one each Dooker, Itchy, Trouble, Snuggler, Hunter, Guardian. Recipes must never request more copies than the pool permits. The random deal must not reserve special roles for bots or humans. A replay may show the Active Set and Hammock after the round, but hidden Hammock identities remain private during play except for the one card Dooker legally views.

If a future player-count mode falls outside four through ten, it requires an explicit recipe revision. The current helper's clamping behavior is not a design rule for unsupported counts.


6. d12 uniqueness and chronological action assignment
-----------------------------------------------------

All d12 results are Burrow Hours. A role with multiple scheduled wakes must end setup with distinct personal wake hours. When a duplicate appears within that player's own schedule, only the duplicate result is rerolled until it becomes unique. Other players may of course share that same hour; collisions between players are a core evidence mechanic and are never rerolled away.

Multi-wake role actions are assigned by chronological wake order, not by the order in which the player physically rolled the dice. Dooker therefore observes on the earliest of its three distinct hours, Glimpses on the middle hour, and performs Hammock peek/optional Trip on the latest hour. Hunter's first and second wake are likewise chronological. Activated Itchy simply has two observation windows; its partial clue is governed separately below.

Raw d12 values are private to the player who owns them unless another role legally learns one. Business inspection reveals exactly one target wake result, not a whole schedule. Public claims about wake hours remain voluntary testimony and are never auto-verified.


7. Bandit Raider selection, including a tied d6 tiebreak
--------------------------------------------------------

The historical Raider is selected from starting Bandits only. Compare the starting Bandits' d12 wake hours. The earliest starting-Bandit wake is Raider. If exactly one starting Bandit owns the earliest hour, no d6 is rolled for Raider selection.

If two or more starting Bandits tie for the earliest hour, only those tied earliest Bandits enter the Raider tiebreak. Each rolls one private d6. The lowest unique d6 among the tied candidates becomes Raider. If the minimum d6 value is shared by more than one tied candidate, discard only that unresolved tiebreak result for those candidates and reroll the still-tied minimum candidates until one unique lowest value exists. Candidates who rolled a strictly higher value in the preceding comparison do not re-enter the tie. This is a repeated use of the same d6, not a new die type or new chance mechanic.

All starting Bandits are then privately told the historical Raider identity so the hostile team shares the same primary asset. The Raider remains player-bound history for the entire round. No later card movement, conversion, protection, reveal, or role claim can transfer Raider status.

This tie procedure should receive deterministic QA fixtures for two-way, three-way, repeated-lowest-tie, and no-tie cases. Reconnect must restore the selected Raider result without asking players to reroll a completed authoritative tiebreak.


8. Same-hour order and visibility boundaries
--------------------------------------------

The same-hour order remains: scheduled players wake and see the co-waker set; Treat State at the start of the window is shown; the historical Raider steals if this is the Raider's theft hour and the treats are still present; theft-witness and conversion triggers resolve; scheduled Starting-Role actions resolve; authorized facts are committed; players return to sleep.

A player who wakes in the theft window can therefore have two related but distinct facts: Treat State was PRESENT at the start of the window, and the treats changed to MISSING during that same window. The claim parser and replay must preserve PRESENT_TO_MISSING as a richer chronology rather than treating PRESENT and MISSING as a contradiction when both refer to different instants inside the same hour.

A sleeping player does not receive another participant's action prompt, target choice, raw die result, or role animation merely because the client is rendering the same clock. Global ambience can communicate that the night continues, but it cannot encode a hidden actor's identity. Authorized co-wakers are the exception: they know who shared the wake because occupancy is part of their evidence.


9. Treat State interval logic
-----------------------------

PRESENT and MISSING are observations, not guilt labels. A credible PRESENT observation at Hour H means the theft had not completed before that observation. A credible MISSING observation at Hour K means the theft completed no later than that observation, subject to same-window order. A witnessed PRESENT_TO_MISSING transition identifies the theft hour exactly for that witness but still does not, by itself, identify which co-waker was Raider when more than one eligible actor shared the window.

The UI may help players display their own observations, but it must not compute a server-endorsed guilt score. Bots may calculate private heuristics from authorized evidence, but public chat should express those as deductions, not as hidden truth. Contradictions should be mechanical and timestamped: a claim of MISSING at Hour 2 conflicts with an authorized later PRESENT at Hour 5; a claim of PRESENT at Hour 8 conflicts with an authorized earlier MISSING at Hour 6. Different-hour changes that follow the legal direction PRESENT to MISSING are not contradictions.


10. Target legality table and server-side validation
---------------------------------------------------

All role target restrictions are authoritative backend rules, not merely disabled buttons. Dooker Glimpse targets one other active player. Dooker Trip, if used, places the viewed Hammock card onto one other active player. Trouble swaps two distinct other active players and never self. Business solo inspection chooses one other active player. Snuggler bonds two distinct other active players. Hunter Hunt Mark targets one other active player. Guardian protects one other active player. Paw Point targets one other active player. A player may never satisfy a two-target action by choosing the same seat twice.

Inactive, disconnected-but-removed, spectator, or non-seat entities are not legal targets. A temporarily disconnected active seat remains a player if the authoritative game still treats that participant as active; reconnect status alone does not erase them from the round. The backend must validate identity, activity, distinctness, and self-exclusion even when the frontend prompt already filters those choices.

Dooker Trip and Business solo inspection are optional. Guardian protection is optional; passing means no player is protected for that resolution. Dooker Glimpse, Trouble's swap, Snuggler's pre-night bond, the Raider theft, and a loyal Hunter's available Hunt Mark are required role actions when their legal prompt exists. If the backend cannot produce a legal required target because the match state is malformed, the round should fail safely rather than silently inventing a self-target or skipping the action as though it were legal.


11. Dooker Glimpse: timestamped allegiance, not Raider detection
---------------------------------------------------------------

Dooker's second chronological wake may Glimpse one other active player. The result is exactly BANDIT-ALIGNED or NOT BANDIT-ALIGNED at that timestamp after same-hour conversion triggers and before later-hour card movement. It is not a scan for Starting Role, Current Card identity, historical Raider status, or final allegiance.

The Glimpse result must be stored with the observed hour. If a known later Dooker Trip or Trouble swap affects that player, the Glimpse remains a true historical fact but may be stale as a description of dawn. Hunter or Trouble conversion before the Glimpse can also make the target Bandit-aligned even if the Current Card is not Bandit. Historical Raider remains Bandit-aligned regardless of card movement.

A Dooker claim such as "Alex was Bandit-aligned at Hour 5" is therefore stronger and more accurate than "Alex is Bandit". Bot reasoning and Quick Role Claim should prefer the timestamped wording. The UI must never translate NOT BANDIT-ALIGNED into SAFE or BANDIT-ALIGNED into RAIDER.


12. Dooker Hammock peek and optional Trip
-----------------------------------------

On Dooker's third chronological wake, Dooker views exactly one of the three Hammock cards. That identity is a private fact. Dooker may then pass or Trip that same viewed Hammock card onto one other active player. The target's Current Card is displaced into the Hammock position. Dooker knows which Hammock position was viewed, the viewed role, whether the Trip occurred, and the target seat. Dooker does not learn the identity of the displaced player card as part of Trip.

The target does not automatically learn that their Current Card was replaced. The Starting Role and scheduled actions do not change. If Dooker viewed a Bandit card and placed it onto a Business-starting player, the target may become Bandit-side under the ordinary Current Card rule without gaining starting-Bandit roster knowledge. If Dooker removes a Bandit Current Card from a non-Raider starting Bandit, that player may become Business-side unless a higher-precedence player-bound status keeps them hostile.

Trip may make Dooker's earlier Glimpse stale, including when Dooker knowingly moves a card that changes the target's allegiance after the scan. That is intended evidence contamination, not a rules error.


13. Trouble conversion and blind swap
-------------------------------------

Trouble wakes once. If Trouble shares the historical Raider's theft window, the conversion trigger resolves before Trouble's Starting-Role action. Trouble becomes In On It, becomes Bandit-side under player-bound precedence, and privately learns the historical Raider identity. That status follows Trouble even if Trouble's Current Card later changes.

Trouble then performs the same blind swap whether loyal or converted: choose two distinct other active players; exchange their Current Cards; learn which two positions were swapped; do not learn either hidden card identity from the swap. Conversion does not turn Trouble's blind swap into an informed swap.

If Trouble does not share the theft window, no conversion occurs. Merely waking after the theft and seeing MISSING is not enough. Merely sharing an hour with a non-Raider starting Bandit is not enough. The trigger is presence in the actual theft window.


14. Business lone-wake inspection
---------------------------------

Business is genuinely alone when no other active player is scheduled to wake in that same Burrow Hour at the start of the wake. A sleeping role that acts later, a card-moved role identity, or a player who would have had a different hour does not change that occupancy fact. If Business is alone, Business may choose one other active player and inspect exactly one of that target's scheduled d12 wake results.

If the target has multiple wakes, the authoritative game selects one of those results uniformly at random. Business does not choose which of the target's wakes to inspect and does not learn how many total wake results the target owns. If the target has one wake, that one result is returned. The inspection is a private hard fact about a wake result, not about the target's role or allegiance.

Legacy payloads that contain a wakeHours array must be normalized down to one result before the UI or bot uses them. The compatibility choice must be stable for the same authoritative event and must not expose the discarded values.


15. Itchy activation and clue delivery
--------------------------------------

Itchy rolls one setup d6. Results 1–4 are Too Itchy: one d12 wake and no special clue. Results 5–6 are Ears Up: two distinct d12 wakes and one partial historical-Raider clue. The activation d6 is private unless voluntarily claimed or revealed after the round.

A normal activated clue contains exactly two active player identities: the historical Raider and one uniformly selected active non-Raider decoy other than Itchy, whenever a legal decoy exists. The app never labels which name is Raider. The clue is tied to historical Raider identity and therefore remains valid even if Current Cards move later.

The clue cannot be generated truthfully until the historical Raider has been selected and the theft has committed. Generation and delivery are distinct. If the theft occurs during or before an Itchy wake at which private facts may be delivered, the clue can be delivered after the theft event is committed. If Itchy's scheduled wakes both occur before the theft, the clue is held privately by the authoritative game and delivered at Morning Business rather than waking Itchy out of Night Lock. This keeps the clue's content correct without creating an unauthorized extra wake.

At four or more valid active players, a normal round should virtually always have a legal non-Raider decoy. A degraded clue is therefore an explicit compatibility or malformed-edge path, not a routine alternate power. If a legal two-name pair cannot be formed, the UI must label the clue DEGRADED and must not present a one-name clue as though it carries the normal exactly-one-of-two guarantee. A legacy timing clue is also degraded and must not be treated by bots as equivalent to the normal candidate pair.


16. Hunter recognition, conversion, and mark timing
---------------------------------------------------

If both Hunter and Guardian are player-held Starting Roles, they recognize one another before Hour 1. Recognition is historical Starting-Role information. Guardian never receives Hunter's later conversion result.

Hunter has two distinct chronological wake hours. The first time Hunter shares a wake with any player whose Starting Role is Bandit, Hunter performs exactly one private conversion d6 check. The trigger uses Starting Role history, not that player's current allegiance at the time. Therefore the trigger can occur even if the starting Bandit's Current Card moved away earlier. If several starting Bandits share that same first applicable Hunter wake, only one conversion check occurs.

On 1–2, Hunter converts to Bandit side and never gains or resolves the loyal Hunt Mark. On 3–6, Hunter remains loyal. If the first applicable overlap occurs on Hunter's second wake and Hunter stays loyal, the conversion trigger resolves before Starting-Role actions in that same hour, so Hunter may set the Hunt Mark during that second wake. If Hunter never overlaps a starting Bandit, Hunter stays loyal and sets the mark on the second wake. If Hunter converted on the first wake, the later second wake remains an observation window but does not grant a mark.

A converted Hunter may continue to be truthfully recognized by Guardian as the starting Hunter. That recognition has become stale trust, not false history.


17. Guardian protection and the zero-capture edge case
------------------------------------------------------

Guardian may protect one other active player after Morning Business and before Guardian's Paw Point is locked. Protection is private until resolution unless the game intentionally reveals it in Results. Guardian may also pass. Protection and Guardian's own vote are separate decisions.

A protected player can still receive Paw Point votes. Those votes remain recorded for replay and may be socially meaningful, but the protected player is removed from vote-capture eligibility for this resolution. Compute the highest vote total only among unprotected eligible targets that actually received at least one valid vote. Catch every unprotected eligible player tied at that highest total.

If every valid vote was cast at the protected player, or if removing the protected target leaves no unprotected target with at least one vote, Paw Point catches nobody. The game does not manufacture a zero-vote "next highest" target. If a protected player and an unprotected player were tied for the apparent lead, remove the protected player and catch the unprotected tied leader if that player has the highest remaining positive total.

Protection never means innocence. It does not block Dooker, Trouble, conversion history, Snuggle Bond, Hunt Mark targeting, or post-game reveal. It modifies Paw Point capture eligibility only.


18. Simultaneous Paw Point and lock semantics
---------------------------------------------

Each active player selects exactly one other active player and locks once. The selection remains private until authoritative resolution. The number of players who have locked may be shown as progress, but target identities and partial tallies must remain hidden. Chat may remain open while players are choosing and locking; discussion does not make votes sequential.

A locked Paw Point cannot be changed. The backend must reject self-votes, nonexistent targets, duplicate votes, and votes from inactive participants even if a malformed client submits them. Simultaneous means resolution waits for the required lock condition or authoritative timeout/host rule; it does not mean every client request arrives at the same millisecond.

Ties are intentional. After Guardian protection eligibility is applied, every eligible player tied at the highest positive vote total is added to the Paw Point catch set. There is no Sheriff, Mayor, leader, captain, host tiebreak vote, first-lock priority, random tiebreak, or d6 vote tiebreak.


19. Secondary resolution order: Paw Point, Hunt Mark, Bond-Reveal
---------------------------------------------------------------

Revision 3.1 makes the post-vote order explicit because Snuggle Bond can react to a valid Hunt Mark catch. First, resolve Paw Point and create the Paw Point catch set after Guardian protection. Second, check Hunter. If Hunter remained loyal, has a valid Hunt Mark, and Hunter is in the Paw Point catch set, evaluate the marked target's allegiance at resolution. If the marked target is Bandit-aligned, add that target to the caught set as a Hunt Mark catch. If Hunter is not caught by Paw Point, the mark does nothing. If Hunter converted, the mark does nothing even if a stale prompt or legacy payload exists.

Third, after the caught set is stable, resolve Snuggle Bond result reveals. For each bond, if at least one bonded partner is in the caught set because of Paw Point or a valid Hunt Mark, the other partner becomes Bond-Revealed in Results. Bond-Reveal does not add that partner to the caught set. It cannot trigger Hunt Mark, cannot satisfy the historical Raider catch requirement, and does not recursively create another catch. If both bonded players are already caught, the bond adds no extra capture.

Fourth, compute the primary winner from the stable caught set. Business wins if and only if the historical Raider is caught by Paw Point or a valid Hunt Mark. Bandit side wins if the historical Raider escapes. A Bond-Revealed historical Raider who was not caught still counts as escaped.

This order separates three concepts that should never be merged in UI text: caught by Paw Point, caught by Hunt Mark, and revealed by bond.


20. Historical Raider and final-win semantics
---------------------------------------------

The headline mystery remains who actually stole the Minnow Treats. Additional Bandit-aligned players can exist because of multiple starting Bandits, Current Card movement, Trouble conversion, or Hunter conversion. Those players can matter tactically, but catching them does not substitute for catching the historical Raider.

The historical Raider can be caught by an eligible top Paw Point result or by a valid loyal-Hunter Hunt Mark if Hunter was caught and the marked Raider is Bandit-aligned at resolution. Guardian protection can prevent Paw Point capture of the Raider for that resolution, but it does not erase Raider history. A Bond-Reveal can expose the Raider in Results but does not count as capture.

Primary winner display must therefore reference the historical Raider's caught state, not the number of Bandit-aligned players caught. Secondary personal outcomes may be listed separately.


21. Public claim grammar and contradiction discipline
-----------------------------------------------------

Quick Role Claim remains a statement builder. It may help a player phrase a Starting Role claim, Current Card claim, wake hour or hours, Treat State at a claimed hour, co-waker, Dooker Glimpse, Business inspection, Trouble swap, Itchy activation/clue, Snuggle Bond, Hunter recognition/mark, or Guardian protection. The generated statement is not authenticated against hidden truth.

Starting Role and Current Card claims are stored independently. A player can truthfully or falsely claim "I started Guardian; my Current Card is Business" without the parser treating those two role names as an automatic contradiction. Starting Role claims determine what wake-count and action capability checks are mechanically relevant. A Current Card claim never grants the claimed Starting Role's wake count.

Legal multi-wake claims may accumulate distinct hours up to the claimed Starting Role's mechanical maximum: Dooker three, activated-claim Itchy two, Hunter two, ordinary one-wake roles one. This cap is a contradiction heuristic, not hidden-role verification. A hostile player may lie within a mechanically plausible pattern. The app should flag only statements that conflict with the public story itself or with authorized observations, not statements merely because hidden server truth disagrees.

Treat State should be stored per claimed hour. PRESENT_TO_MISSING is a valid same-hour chronology. The parser should avoid converting incomplete language into certainty. "I think the theft was around Hour 6" is an inference, not a hard Treat State claim.


22. Bot information firewall and parity tests
--------------------------------------------

Bots must receive enough information to perform their role but no more than a human in the same seat. This requirement applies to action choice, chat, challenge generation, voting, protection, and silence. A bot's internal implementation may call an authoritative API, but the response must be filtered before reasoning if it contains server-only fields.

Required bot parity tests now include: a moved Current Card that the bot is not entitled to see; an allegiance field that exists server-side but has not been privately revealed; another player's raw d6/d12; a hidden Hammock identity not viewed by Dooker; a Guardian-Hunter recognition that later becomes stale; a Dooker Glimpse that becomes stale after known movement; an Itchy candidate pair; a Trouble conversion; a Hunter conversion; and a reconnect after hidden events occurred. The bot must produce the same legal set of claims and action choices it could have produced from the equivalent human-facing facts.

Hostile bot logic may protect a known historical Raider because starting Bandits are entitled to that identity. It may sacrifice a non-Raider ally if doing so helps Raider survival. It may not know the Raider merely because the backend has `raiderId`. Loyal Itchy voting may be constrained to a valid non-degraded two-name clue because Itchy is entitled to that pair. Guardian must not auto-protect starting Hunter because recognition does not certify current loyalty. Converted Hunter must not use Hunt Mark.


23. Reconnect, persistence, and anti-leak behavior
-------------------------------------------------

Reconnect reconstructs only the information the reconnecting participant is currently authorized to know. It may restore own Starting Role, own completed wake schedule, own observations, own legal private facts, own locked vote state, and public chat. It may restore a pending action if the action is still legally pending. It must not replay another participant's private prompt, raw die animation, target choice, hidden Current Card, or role reveal.

A reconnect after Dooker Trip should not suddenly tell a target that the target's card moved if the target did not know before disconnecting. A reconnect after Hunter conversion may restore Hunter's own conversion result because Hunter is entitled to it, while Guardian still receives no conversion update. A reconnect after Trouble conversion may restore Trouble's In On It status and Raider identity. Reconnect cannot be used as a hidden-information oracle by intentionally refreshing the client.

Post-game replay is different. Once the game is in Results and host privacy settings allow it, replay may expose historical events for explanation. The replay must label them as post-game knowledge rather than implying they were visible in real time.


24. Result terminology and result cards
---------------------------------------

Results should reveal each player's Starting Role history and final Current Card when available, plus player-bound statuses relevant to explaining the outcome. Result text should use separate verbs: CAUGHT for Paw Point or valid Hunt Mark; BOND-REVEALED for a Snuggle Bond result reveal; CONVERTED for Hunter or Trouble status; PROTECTED for Guardian's procedural choice; RAIDER for the historical thief.

A player who is Bond-Revealed but not caught must not receive the same visual treatment as a caught player. A historical Raider who is only Bond-Revealed is still an escaped Raider for the primary result. A non-Raider Bandit caught by Paw Point is caught but does not independently satisfy Business's primary objective. If Hunter's mark catches a Bandit-aligned target, the result should identify Hunt Mark as the capture source.

Final Current Card reveal does not retroactively change which Starting-Role actions happened. The result screen should make card movement understandable as a transition, for example "Started: Guardian · Final: Business," without implying the participant performed Business's night action.


25. Implementation metadata is descriptive, not authoritative
-------------------------------------------------------------

Some package metadata may retain an older string such as `dossier-r2-aligned` even when the surrounding implementation and tests are Revision 3. That version label is metadata drift, not permission to apply Revision 2 semantics. Revision 3.1 defines the intended gameplay. Future maintenance should update stale metadata strings so audit tools do not produce false alarms, but behavior is judged by the actual state contract and tests.

Likewise, a utility such as a generic simultaneous-vote helper may be less restrictive than the complete game rule. The authoritative server still has to reject self-votes and illegal targets. A helper that can technically accept an illegal object is not a gameplay rule unless the backend and dossier intentionally permit it.


26. Expanded deterministic QA matrix
------------------------------------

In addition to every role's existing seeded QA cases, the package should maintain cross-system fixtures for the newly explicit edges. Raider selection: no tie, two-way earliest-hour tie, repeated equal-low d6, three-way tie. Card uncertainty: Dooker Trip onto a starting Business, Trouble swap involving a starting Bandit, target remains unaware until Results, bot receives no extra knowledge. Dooker: Glimpse before movement, Glimpse after Trouble conversion in the same hour, Glimpse that becomes stale later. Itchy: normal two-name clue, clue delivery when both Itchy wakes precede theft, degraded compatibility input.

Hunter/Guardian: first-wake conversion, second-wake conversion followed by no mark if converted, second-wake loyal check followed by mark in the same hour, no Bandit overlap and ordinary second-wake mark, Guardian protects leading suspect, Guardian passes, all votes land on protected target and nobody is caught. Voting: tied top catch, protected tied leader removed, self-vote rejected, duplicate vote rejected, chat remains available while vote targets remain private.

Secondary results: Hunter caught and valid Bandit-aligned mark catches target; Hunter not caught so mark does nothing; converted Hunter mark suppressed; Hunt Mark catch activates Snuggle Bond reveal; Bond-Revealed Raider does not satisfy Business win; Hunt Mark-caught Raider does satisfy Business win. Reconnect: each private fact restores only to entitled player. Every test should assert hidden state, authorized private state, public state, bot-visible state, and result state separately.


27. Balance consequences of these clarifications
-----------------------------------------------

These clarifications do not intentionally buff or nerf a role; they remove accidental information. Hiding moved Current Cards until Results protects the card-movement mystery and prevents targets from becoming involuntary perfect witnesses. Preventing bots from using unrevealed current allegiance avoids an artificial bot advantage. Explicit Hunt Mark-before-Bond-Reveal ordering lets the designed secondary effects compose without turning reveal into capture. Explicit zero-capture Guardian resolution prevents the engine from selecting a player who received no votes. Recursive lowest-only Raider tiebreak removes an undefined setup branch without adding strategic complexity.

Playtests should still watch the same core measures: how many plausible Raider narratives survive into Morning Business; whether Treat State chronology matters; whether one special role collapses the mystery too early; whether card movement creates interpretable uncertainty rather than noise; whether hostile players can plausibly imitate ordinary Business testimony; whether players understand historical facts versus current state; and whether bots are explainable from the same evidence as humans.


28. Revision 3.1 implementation checklist
----------------------------------------

A conforming implementation should be able to answer YES to every statement below. Lobby seats are role-neutral. Starting Cards are dealt randomly from the correct Active Set and three cards remain in Hammock. Starting Role is immutable. Multi-wake d12s are personally unique. Earliest starting Bandit is Raider, with a fully defined recursive lowest-d6 tiebreak if necessary. Same-hour order is stable. Treat State preserves PRESENT_TO_MISSING. Dooker Glimpse is timestamped current allegiance. Dooker Trip does not reveal the displaced card. Trouble converts before swapping and learns Raider only on theft-window overlap. Business receives one random target wake result, not a schedule. Itchy's normal clue is exactly Raider plus one legal decoy and can be deferred to Morning if theft occurs after both Itchy wakes. Hunter converts only on the first starting-Bandit overlap check and converted Hunter has no mark. Guardian protection can be passed and can produce a zero-capture Paw Point if all votes become ineligible.

Role targets exclude self and enforce distinctness where required. Vote targets exclude self. All eligible tied positive top totals are caught. Paw Point resolves before Hunt Mark; Hunt Mark resolves before Bond-Reveal; Bond-Reveal never adds a catch. Primary Business victory checks historical Raider caught status only after the catch set is stable. Moved Current Cards and ordinary current-card-derived allegiance are not automatically revealed before Results. Bots cannot use hidden server-only fields. Reconnect restores only authorized information. Results distinguish caught, converted, protected, Raider, and Bond-Revealed states. Public claims remain unauthenticated testimony.

These contracts are the bridge between the 96,000-plus-word role/interactions analysis and executable code. They are deliberately precise enough that a human moderator, frontend, backend, automated test, and role bot should resolve the same seeded round the same way without needing to guess what an earlier sentence meant.


PART V — 28-PAIR ROLE INTERACTION DOSSIER
=========================================

This section is deliberately over twenty thousand words. It analyzes every unordered pair among the
eight roles. The purpose is not to say that only two roles can be active at a time; it is to isolate
how each pair changes evidence, trust, card state, conversion, claims, and voting. Multi-role
scenarios should be built by composing these pair rules while preserving the global event order.


INTERACTION 01: BANDIT x DOOKER
-------------------------------

Pair thesis. Dooker can create the cleanest hard-allegiance pressure on Bandit, but Glimpse is about
current allegiance at a timestamp while Raider identity is historical. Bandit therefore attacks the
inference layer rather than the raw result. Bandit's anchor is: Starting Bandits know the starting
Bandit roster. The earliest starting-Bandit wake becomes the historical Raider and must steal the
Minnow Treats in that window. Other Bandits are accomplices whose job is to keep the Raider out of
the final caught set. Dooker's anchor is: Dooker wakes three times. First chronological wake is
observation. Second chronological wake grants one Glimpse of a chosen player’s current allegiance as
Bandit-aligned or Not Bandit-aligned. Third chronological wake lets Dooker view one Hammock card and
optionally Trip it onto one player, exchanging that player’s Current Card without seeing the
displaced card. The interaction is healthy only if those two mechanics create more interpretable
evidence rather than a hidden exception that players cannot reason about.

Cheese Thief lens. Treat the pair as two nodes in a wake-time evidence graph. Bandit contributes 1
d12; Dooker contributes 3 distinct d12s. Test no-overlap, ordinary overlap, and theft-window overlap
configurations. In each configuration, record who can truthfully report PRESENT, MISSING, co-wakers,
and any state change. The pair should preserve the central before/after puzzle even when role
actions add information or change allegiance.

Among Us lens. Ask which previously reliable tell this pair makes ambiguous. Bandit's special
strength is shared hostile knowledge and the fact that the theft is a historical event that card
movement cannot erase; Dooker's is broad temporal coverage plus one hard-but-binary scan and one
controlled source of card-state uncertainty. The design goal is not to make evidence useless. It is
to force the table to add one more question before treating a signal as conclusive. If the pair
makes every observation meaningless, it is too noisy; if one role simply prints the other's guilt,
it is too deterministic.

Information asymmetry. Bandit can directly know own wake hour; starting Bandit roster; Raider
identity; Treat State and co-wakers at the Bandit’s own wake; any later facts gained through card
movement or public discussion. Dooker can directly know three wake windows, three Treat States/co-
waker sets, one binary allegiance result at a specific moment, one known Hammock card, and exact
knowledge of whether/where the viewed Hammock card was moved. Neither player inherits the other's
private facts merely because the roles interact. A bot, reconnect path, spectator, or result preview
must enforce the same boundary. Public statements are claims until independently corroborated or
revealed in post-game results.

Claim dynamics. A typical Bandit claim may involve wake hour, Treat State, co-wakers, an ordinary
Business claim, or a carefully chosen special-role claim when plausible; the most durable cover
keeps true chronology and changes only the incriminating implication. A typical Dooker claim may
involve can reveal all three hours or stagger information, can disclose Glimpse without immediately
revealing which wake produced it, and can truthfully describe the viewed Hammock card while
remaining uncertain about the displaced card. During Morning Business, the pair should generate
cross-examination rather than forced confession. Useful questions are whether claimed hours match
witnessed co-wakers, whether Treat State is chronologically possible, whether a card moved between
the relevant timestamps, and whether a claimed trust relation is historical or current.

State-change discipline. If Dooker or Trouble moves either player's Current Card, both Bandit and
Dooker still resolve scheduled Starting Role actions. If Trouble or Hunter has a player-bound
allegiance conversion, that conversion stays with the player. If one of the pair is historical
Raider, Raider status stays with that player. This rule prevents the pair from creating retroactive
contradictions and makes the Trail of Trouble replay intelligible.

Vote pressure. Bandit's voting doctrine is: keep the historical Raider out of every top eligible Paw
Point position, including ties; sacrificing a non-Raider Bandit, converted Trouble, or converted
Hunter is acceptable if it protects the Raider. Dooker's voting doctrine is: vote for the player
whose total story best fits the Raider timeline after accounting for stale Glimpse information and
card movement; do not mechanically vote every Bandit-aligned Glimpse target if the historical Raider
evidence points elsewhere. Before lock, Guardian may remove one other player from vote-capture
eligibility. The pair must be tested under a clean plurality, a two-way tie, a three-way tie, and a
case where the protected player would otherwise lead. Secondary Bond-Reveal or Hunt Mark effects
occur only after the primary Paw Point catch set is known.

Counterplay for Bandit. The most important risk posed by Dooker is not simply its power text; it is
how that power can change the interpretation of Bandit's evidence. Bandit should respond by
timestamping facts, keeping the Treat State bracket visible, and refusing to let a single role claim
replace the historical-Raider question. In a hostile Bandit state, the best counterplay is usually a
plausible reinterpretation, not an impossible denial of facts another player personally saw.

Counterplay for Dooker. The most important risk posed by Bandit is that its story may be partly
true. Dooker should avoid binary reasoning such as 'one lie means every statement is false' or 'one
confirmed fact clears the whole player.' Instead compare independent evidence layers: hour, Treat
State, co-wakers, starting-role capability, Current Card movement, allegiance-changing events, and
voting incentives.

Scenario A: Bandit wakes before Dooker, and the raid occurs between their first relevant
observations. Bandit reports PRESENT; Dooker reports MISSING. This brackets the raid without proving
either role claim. Add one public claim from a third player that conflicts with only one side. The
pair should be able to explain why chronology carries more weight than confidence.

Scenario B: Bandit and Dooker share the theft window. Treat State changes during the window.
Determine whether either role has a conversion trigger, whether the historical Raider is actually
one of them, and which role actions occur after the theft trigger. The rules must make event order
explicit enough that both players can later give internally consistent but strategically different
stories.

Scenario C: a Dooker Trip or Trouble swap changes one participant's Current Card after an earlier
observation. Morning Business now contains a true statement about Starting Role and a different true
statement about current state. The pair passes only if players can reconcile both without treating
one as an automatic lie.

Balance watch. Bandit failure risk: the Treat State timeline can bracket the raid; a witnessed
theft, Dooker Glimpse, Itchy candidate clue, Business die inspection, or contradiction can narrow
the Raider sharply. Dooker failure risk: three wakes make Dooker easier to cross-check; the Trip can
accidentally help Bandits, can make Dooker’s earlier Glimpse stale, and can create false confidence
if players conflate current allegiance with historical Raider identity. Track whether the pair
causes automatic clears, automatic guilt, or excessive narrative branching. The target is
constrained ambiguity: two or three plausible stories at the start of Morning Business, with enough
mechanically grounded questions to reduce that set before Paw Point.

Bot behavior. A Bandit bot follows: protect the historical Raider, avoid voting known allies unless
sacrificial distancing materially reduces Raider risk, and prefer frame targets whose public timing
already contains contradictions. A Dooker bot follows: separate historical theft suspicion from
current-allegiance suspicion, preserve timestamps on Glimpse/Trip, and never invent knowledge of the
card displaced by Trip. Neither bot may read the other's hidden state from the authoritative server
merely because the simulation has access to it. Their conversation should be reproducible from
authorized facts plus parsed public claims. This is particularly important in solo play, where
omniscient bots would destroy the deduction game.

QA checklist. Seed at least six pair fixtures: no overlap; ordinary overlap before raid; ordinary
overlap after raid; theft-window overlap; one card movement; one Guardian-protected vote. For each
fixture, save starting cards, Current Cards after every move, d12 schedules, relevant d6 results,
Treat State transitions, player-bound statuses, private fact packets, public claims, Paw Point
drafts, locked votes, protection, catch set, secondary effects, and primary winner. The same event
log must replay to the same result.


INTERACTION 02: BANDIT x ITCHY
------------------------------

Pair thesis. Itchy can force the Raider into a two-name box. Bandit counterplay is to make the
innocent candidate look mechanically impossible to clear, not to pretend the candidate list never
existed. Bandit's anchor is: Starting Bandits know the starting Bandit roster. The earliest
starting-Bandit wake becomes the historical Raider and must steal the Minnow Treats in that window.
Other Bandits are accomplices whose job is to keep the Raider out of the final caught set. Itchy's
anchor is: Itchy always receives normal wake evidence. On d6 5–6, Itchy gains a second distinct wake
and, after the raid is committed, receives a partial two-player clue containing the historical
Raider and one valid non-Raider decoy whenever the game can construct such a pair. The interaction
is healthy only if those two mechanics create more interpretable evidence rather than a hidden
exception that players cannot reason about.

Cheese Thief lens. Treat the pair as two nodes in a wake-time evidence graph. Bandit contributes 1
d12; Itchy contributes 1 d12 normally; 2 distinct d12s on activation. Test no-overlap, ordinary
overlap, and theft-window overlap configurations. In each configuration, record who can truthfully
report PRESENT, MISSING, co-wakers, and any state change. The pair should preserve the central
before/after puzzle even when role actions add information or change allegiance.

Among Us lens. Ask which previously reliable tell this pair makes ambiguous. Bandit's special
strength is shared hostile knowledge and the fact that the theft is a historical event that card
movement cannot erase; Itchy's is on activated nights, Itchy converts a broad mystery into a
constrained suspect pair while still requiring social deduction. The design goal is not to make
evidence useless. It is to force the table to add one more question before treating a signal as
conclusive. If the pair makes every observation meaningless, it is too noisy; if one role simply
prints the other's guilt, it is too deterministic.

Information asymmetry. Bandit can directly know own wake hour; starting Bandit roster; Raider
identity; Treat State and co-wakers at the Bandit’s own wake; any later facts gained through card
movement or public discussion. Itchy can directly know activation result; one or two wake windows;
Treat State/co-wakers; if activated, exactly two clue candidates with the guarantee that one is the
historical Raider when a valid decoy exists. Neither player inherits the other's private facts
merely because the roles interact. A bot, reconnect path, spectator, or result preview must enforce
the same boundary. Public statements are claims until independently corroborated or revealed in
post-game results.

Claim dynamics. A typical Bandit claim may involve wake hour, Treat State, co-wakers, an ordinary
Business claim, or a carefully chosen special-role claim when plausible; the most durable cover
keeps true chronology and changes only the incriminating implication. A typical Itchy claim may
involve can truthfully report no activation, can reveal one or both wake windows, and can reveal
candidate pair without claiming certainty about which candidate is Raider. During Morning Business,
the pair should generate cross-examination rather than forced confession. Useful questions are
whether claimed hours match witnessed co-wakers, whether Treat State is chronologically possible,
whether a card moved between the relevant timestamps, and whether a claimed trust relation is
historical or current.

State-change discipline. If Dooker or Trouble moves either player's Current Card, both Bandit and
Itchy still resolve scheduled Starting Role actions. If Trouble or Hunter has a player-bound
allegiance conversion, that conversion stays with the player. If one of the pair is historical
Raider, Raider status stays with that player. This rule prevents the pair from creating retroactive
contradictions and makes the Trail of Trouble replay intelligible.

Vote pressure. Bandit's voting doctrine is: keep the historical Raider out of every top eligible Paw
Point position, including ties; sacrificing a non-Raider Bandit, converted Trouble, or converted
Hunter is acceptable if it protects the Raider. Itchy's voting doctrine is: use the clue to
constrain rather than dictate. Prefer the candidate whose wake story conflicts with the public theft
bracket, but remain willing to vote outside the pair only if a rules edge case invalidated the
normal guarantee. Before lock, Guardian may remove one other player from vote-capture eligibility.
The pair must be tested under a clean plurality, a two-way tie, a three-way tie, and a case where
the protected player would otherwise lead. Secondary Bond-Reveal or Hunt Mark effects occur only
after the primary Paw Point catch set is known.

Counterplay for Bandit. The most important risk posed by Itchy is not simply its power text; it is
how that power can change the interpretation of Bandit's evidence. Bandit should respond by
timestamping facts, keeping the Treat State bracket visible, and refusing to let a single role claim
replace the historical-Raider question. In a hostile Bandit state, the best counterplay is usually a
plausible reinterpretation, not an impossible denial of facts another player personally saw.

Counterplay for Itchy. The most important risk posed by Bandit is that its story may be partly true.
Itchy should avoid binary reasoning such as 'one lie means every statement is false' or 'one
confirmed fact clears the whole player.' Instead compare independent evidence layers: hour, Treat
State, co-wakers, starting-role capability, Current Card movement, allegiance-changing events, and
voting incentives.

Scenario A: Bandit wakes before Itchy, and the raid occurs between their first relevant
observations. Bandit reports PRESENT; Itchy reports MISSING. This brackets the raid without proving
either role claim. Add one public claim from a third player that conflicts with only one side. The
pair should be able to explain why chronology carries more weight than confidence.

Scenario B: Bandit and Itchy share the theft window. Treat State changes during the window.
Determine whether either role has a conversion trigger, whether the historical Raider is actually
one of them, and which role actions occur after the theft trigger. The rules must make event order
explicit enough that both players can later give internally consistent but strategically different
stories.

Scenario C: a Dooker Trip or Trouble swap changes one participant's Current Card after an earlier
observation. Morning Business now contains a true statement about Starting Role and a different true
statement about current state. The pair passes only if players can reconcile both without treating
one as an automatic lie.

Balance watch. Bandit failure risk: the Treat State timeline can bracket the raid; a witnessed
theft, Dooker Glimpse, Itchy candidate clue, Business die inspection, or contradiction can narrow
the Raider sharply. Itchy failure risk: activation is uncommon, candidate evidence can be framed,
and Itchy becomes an attractive Bandit target for contradiction or false counterclaims because the
clue is powerful but not self-verifying. Track whether the pair causes automatic clears, automatic
guilt, or excessive narrative branching. The target is constrained ambiguity: two or three plausible
stories at the start of Morning Business, with enough mechanically grounded questions to reduce that
set before Paw Point.

Bot behavior. A Bandit bot follows: protect the historical Raider, avoid voting known allies unless
sacrificial distancing materially reduces Raider risk, and prefer frame targets whose public timing
already contains contradictions. A Itchy bot follows: never turn a two-candidate clue into an exact
accusation; record which evidence is ordinary wake evidence and which evidence comes from
activation. Neither bot may read the other's hidden state from the authoritative server merely
because the simulation has access to it. Their conversation should be reproducible from authorized
facts plus parsed public claims. This is particularly important in solo play, where omniscient bots
would destroy the deduction game.

QA checklist. Seed at least six pair fixtures: no overlap; ordinary overlap before raid; ordinary
overlap after raid; theft-window overlap; one card movement; one Guardian-protected vote. For each
fixture, save starting cards, Current Cards after every move, d12 schedules, relevant d6 results,
Treat State transitions, player-bound statuses, private fact packets, public claims, Paw Point
drafts, locked votes, protection, catch set, secondary effects, and primary winner. The same event
log must replay to the same result.


INTERACTION 03: BANDIT x TROUBLE
--------------------------------

Pair thesis. This is the game’s most direct Cheese Thief Follower echo. If Trouble witnesses the
raid, a formerly Business-side role becomes a knowing accomplice, giving Bandit a new ally without
changing the eight-role roster. Bandit's anchor is: Starting Bandits know the starting Bandit
roster. The earliest starting-Bandit wake becomes the historical Raider and must steal the Minnow
Treats in that window. Other Bandits are accomplices whose job is to keep the Raider out of the
final caught set. Trouble's anchor is: At Trouble’s wake, Trouble blindly swaps the Current Cards of
two distinct other players. If Trouble is awake in the exact theft window, Trouble becomes In On It
before the swap resolves and receives the historical Raider identity as part of that conversion. The
interaction is healthy only if those two mechanics create more interpretable evidence rather than a
hidden exception that players cannot reason about.

Cheese Thief lens. Treat the pair as two nodes in a wake-time evidence graph. Bandit contributes 1
d12; Trouble contributes 1 d12. Test no-overlap, ordinary overlap, and theft-window overlap
configurations. In each configuration, record who can truthfully report PRESENT, MISSING, co-wakers,
and any state change. The pair should preserve the central before/after puzzle even when role
actions add information or change allegiance.

Among Us lens. Ask which previously reliable tell this pair makes ambiguous. Bandit's special
strength is shared hostile knowledge and the fact that the theft is a historical event that card
movement cannot erase; Trouble's is creates legitimate card-state ambiguity and, on theft-window
collision, can become a thematically clean Follower-like accomplice without adding a ninth role. The
design goal is not to make evidence useless. It is to force the table to add one more question
before treating a signal as conclusive. If the pair makes every observation meaningless, it is too
noisy; if one role simply prints the other's guilt, it is too deterministic.

Information asymmetry. Bandit can directly know own wake hour; starting Bandit roster; Raider
identity; Treat State and co-wakers at the Bandit’s own wake; any later facts gained through card
movement or public discussion. Trouble can directly know own wake evidence; exact identities of the
two positions swapped; no knowledge of either swapped card; if converted, the fact of conversion and
historical Raider identity. Neither player inherits the other's private facts merely because the
roles interact. A bot, reconnect path, spectator, or result preview must enforce the same boundary.
Public statements are claims until independently corroborated or revealed in post-game results.

Claim dynamics. A typical Bandit claim may involve wake hour, Treat State, co-wakers, an ordinary
Business claim, or a carefully chosen special-role claim when plausible; the most durable cover
keeps true chronology and changes only the incriminating implication. A typical Trouble claim may
involve can truthfully reveal the swap while withholding allegiance change; a loyal Trouble can use
swap facts to explain why role claims may no longer match Current Cards; a converted Trouble can
exploit the same uncertainty to shield the Raider. During Morning Business, the pair should generate
cross-examination rather than forced confession. Useful questions are whether claimed hours match
witnessed co-wakers, whether Treat State is chronologically possible, whether a card moved between
the relevant timestamps, and whether a claimed trust relation is historical or current.

State-change discipline. If Dooker or Trouble moves either player's Current Card, both Bandit and
Trouble still resolve scheduled Starting Role actions. If Trouble or Hunter has a player-bound
allegiance conversion, that conversion stays with the player. If one of the pair is historical
Raider, Raider status stays with that player. This rule prevents the pair from creating retroactive
contradictions and makes the Trail of Trouble replay intelligible.

Vote pressure. Bandit's voting doctrine is: keep the historical Raider out of every top eligible Paw
Point position, including ties; sacrificing a non-Raider Bandit, converted Trouble, or converted
Hunter is acceptable if it protects the Raider. Trouble's voting doctrine is: loyal Trouble votes
for the historical Raider; converted Trouble protects the historical Raider even if that means
accepting personal suspicion. Trouble’s own capture never substitutes for capturing the Raider..
Before lock, Guardian may remove one other player from vote-capture eligibility. The pair must be
tested under a clean plurality, a two-way tie, a three-way tie, and a case where the protected
player would otherwise lead. Secondary Bond-Reveal or Hunt Mark effects occur only after the primary
Paw Point catch set is known.

Counterplay for Bandit. The most important risk posed by Trouble is not simply its power text; it is
how that power can change the interpretation of Bandit's evidence. Bandit should respond by
timestamping facts, keeping the Treat State bracket visible, and refusing to let a single role claim
replace the historical-Raider question. In a hostile Bandit state, the best counterplay is usually a
plausible reinterpretation, not an impossible denial of facts another player personally saw.

Counterplay for Trouble. The most important risk posed by Bandit is that its story may be partly
true. Trouble should avoid binary reasoning such as 'one lie means every statement is false' or 'one
confirmed fact clears the whole player.' Instead compare independent evidence layers: hour, Treat
State, co-wakers, starting-role capability, Current Card movement, allegiance-changing events, and
voting incentives.

Scenario A: Bandit wakes before Trouble, and the raid occurs between their first relevant
observations. Bandit reports PRESENT; Trouble reports MISSING. This brackets the raid without
proving either role claim. Add one public claim from a third player that conflicts with only one
side. The pair should be able to explain why chronology carries more weight than confidence.

Scenario B: Bandit and Trouble share the theft window. Treat State changes during the window.
Determine whether either role has a conversion trigger, whether the historical Raider is actually
one of them, and which role actions occur after the theft trigger. The rules must make event order
explicit enough that both players can later give internally consistent but strategically different
stories.

Scenario C: a Dooker Trip or Trouble swap changes one participant's Current Card after an earlier
observation. Morning Business now contains a true statement about Starting Role and a different true
statement about current state. The pair passes only if players can reconcile both without treating
one as an automatic lie.

Balance watch. Bandit failure risk: the Treat State timeline can bracket the raid; a witnessed
theft, Dooker Glimpse, Itchy candidate clue, Business die inspection, or contradiction can narrow
the Raider sharply. Trouble failure risk: blindness is real; Trouble can accidentally move a Bandit
card, disrupt a useful Business role, or produce a swap that later makes Trouble look responsible
for contradictions it could not predict. Track whether the pair causes automatic clears, automatic
guilt, or excessive narrative branching. The target is constrained ambiguity: two or three plausible
stories at the start of Morning Business, with enough mechanically grounded questions to reduce that
set before Paw Point.

Bot behavior. A Bandit bot follows: protect the historical Raider, avoid voting known allies unless
sacrificial distancing materially reduces Raider risk, and prefer frame targets whose public timing
already contains contradictions. A Trouble bot follows: maintain a strict distinction between swap-
position knowledge and card-identity ignorance. If converted, the bot may coordinate strategically
from known Raider identity but cannot fabricate having seen the swapped cards.. Neither bot may read
the other's hidden state from the authoritative server merely because the simulation has access to
it. Their conversation should be reproducible from authorized facts plus parsed public claims. This
is particularly important in solo play, where omniscient bots would destroy the deduction game.

QA checklist. Seed at least six pair fixtures: no overlap; ordinary overlap before raid; ordinary
overlap after raid; theft-window overlap; one card movement; one Guardian-protected vote. For each
fixture, save starting cards, Current Cards after every move, d12 schedules, relevant d6 results,
Treat State transitions, player-bound statuses, private fact packets, public claims, Paw Point
drafts, locked votes, protection, catch set, secondary effects, and primary winner. The same event
log must replay to the same result.


INTERACTION 04: BANDIT x BUSINESS
---------------------------------

Pair thesis. This pairing is the baseline theft-timeline contest: Bandit tries to occupy a plausible
ordinary claim while Business stitches present/missing observations and inspected wake results into
a bracket. Bandit's anchor is: Starting Bandits know the starting Bandit roster. The earliest
starting-Bandit wake becomes the historical Raider and must steal the Minnow Treats in that window.
Other Bandits are accomplices whose job is to keep the Raider out of the final caught set.
Business's anchor is: Business is the ordinary evidence role. It sees Treat State and co-wakers at
its single wake. If it wakes alone, it may inspect exactly one wake result belonging to one chosen
other player; for a multi-wake target, the app reveals one randomly selected result and does not
reveal how many additional wake results the target has. The interaction is healthy only if those two
mechanics create more interpretable evidence rather than a hidden exception that players cannot
reason about.

Cheese Thief lens. Treat the pair as two nodes in a wake-time evidence graph. Bandit contributes 1
d12; Business contributes 1 d12. Test no-overlap, ordinary overlap, and theft-window overlap
configurations. In each configuration, record who can truthfully report PRESENT, MISSING, co-wakers,
and any state change. The pair should preserve the central before/after puzzle even when role
actions add information or change allegiance.

Among Us lens. Ask which previously reliable tell this pair makes ambiguous. Bandit's special
strength is shared hostile knowledge and the fact that the theft is a historical event that card
movement cannot erase; Business's is forms the baseline evidence network against which special-role
claims and hostile stories can be tested. The design goal is not to make evidence useless. It is to
force the table to add one more question before treating a signal as conclusive. If the pair makes
every observation meaningless, it is too noisy; if one role simply prints the other's guilt, it is
too deterministic.

movement or public discussion. Business can directly know own wake hour; Treat State; co-wakers;

scan. Neither player inherits the other's private facts merely because the roles interact. A bot,
reconnect path, spectator, or result preview must enforce the same boundary. Public statements are
claims until independently corroborated or revealed in post-game results.

Claim dynamics. A typical Bandit claim may involve wake hour, Treat State, co-wakers, an ordinary
Business claim, or a carefully chosen special-role claim when plausible; the most durable cover
keeps true chronology and changes only the incriminating implication. A typical Business claim may
involve simple claims about hour, Treat State, co-wakers, and optional inspected result. Because
Bandits can imitate this structure, credibility must come from consistency and corroboration rather
than uniqueness. During Morning Business, the pair should generate cross-examination rather than
forced confession. Useful questions are whether claimed hours match witnessed co-wakers, whether
Treat State is chronologically possible, whether a card moved between the relevant timestamps, and
whether a claimed trust relation is historical or current.

Business still resolve scheduled Starting Role actions. If Trouble or Hunter has a player-bound
allegiance conversion, that conversion stays with the player. If one of the pair is historical
Raider, Raider status stays with that player. This rule prevents the pair from creating retroactive
contradictions and makes the Trail of Trouble replay intelligible.

Point position, including ties; sacrificing a non-Raider Bandit, converted Trouble, or converted
Hunter is acceptable if it protects the Raider. Business's voting doctrine is: vote from the
reconstructed theft bracket and contradiction network, not from role glamour. A plain Business claim
that fits two other observations can be stronger than a flashy special-role claim that cannot be
reconciled.. Before lock, Guardian may remove one other player from vote-capture eligibility. The
pair must be tested under a clean plurality, a two-way tie, a three-way tie, and a case where the
protected player would otherwise lead. Secondary Bond-Reveal or Hunt Mark effects occur only after
the primary Paw Point catch set is known.

is how that power can change the interpretation of Bandit's evidence. Bandit should respond by

replace the historical-Raider question. In a hostile Bandit state, the best counterplay is usually a
plausible reinterpretation, not an impossible denial of facts another player personally saw.

Counterplay for Business. The most important risk posed by Bandit is that its story may be partly
true. Business should avoid binary reasoning such as 'one lie means every statement is false' or

Treat State, co-wakers, starting-role capability, Current Card movement, allegiance-changing events,

Scenario C: a Dooker Trip or Trouble swap changes one participant's Current Card after an earlier
observation. Morning Business now contains a true statement about Starting Role and a different true

log must replay to the same result.


INTERACTION 05: BANDIT x SNUGGLER
---------------------------------

Pair thesis. The bond can make ordinary cooperation look like hostile coordination and hostile
coordination look socially innocent. Neither side should treat bond as alignment proof. Bandit's
anchor is: Starting Bandits know the starting Bandit roster. The earliest starting-Bandit wake
becomes the historical Raider and must steal the Minnow Treats in that window. Other Bandits are
accomplices whose job is to keep the Raider out of the final caught set. Snuggler's anchor is:
Before Hour 1, Snuggler chooses two distinct other players to become Snuggle-Bonded. The bonded
players learn each other’s identity as partners but not each other’s roles. Bond status is player-
bound. If one bonded player is caught by vote or a valid Hunt Mark, the other becomes Bond-Revealed
in the final resolution; Bond-Reveal exposes identity/current card for results but is not itself a
Paw Point capture. The interaction is healthy only if those two mechanics create more interpretable
evidence rather than a hidden exception that players cannot reason about.

Cheese Thief lens. Treat the pair as two nodes in a wake-time evidence graph. Bandit contributes 1
d12; Snuggler contributes 1 d12. Test no-overlap, ordinary overlap, and theft-window overlap
configurations. In each configuration, record who can truthfully report PRESENT, MISSING, co-wakers,
and any state change. The pair should preserve the central before/after puzzle even when role
actions add information or change allegiance.

strength is shared hostile knowledge and the fact that the theft is a historical event that card
movement cannot erase; Snuggler's is creates a human trust channel that is mechanically real but
alignment-neutral, making social alliances less diagnostic and producing useful ambiguity without
adding another hostile faction. The design goal is not to make evidence useless. It is to force the
table to add one more question before treating a signal as conclusive. If the pair makes every
observation meaningless, it is too noisy; if one role simply prints the other's guilt, it is too
deterministic.

identity; Treat State and co-wakers at the Bandit’s own wake; any later facts gained through card
movement or public discussion. Snuggler can directly know the selected bonded pair; own normal wake
evidence; no alignment information about the pair unless obtained elsewhere. Neither player inherits
the other's private facts merely because the roles interact. A bot, reconnect path, spectator, or
result preview must enforce the same boundary. Public statements are claims until independently
corroborated or revealed in post-game results.

Business claim, or a carefully chosen special-role claim when plausible; the most durable cover
keeps true chronology and changes only the incriminating implication. A typical Snuggler claim may
involve can reveal or conceal the pair. Bonded players may explain cooperation by claiming the bond,
but a bond claim never clears either player because one or both may be hostile. During Morning
Business, the pair should generate cross-examination rather than forced confession. Useful questions
are whether claimed hours match witnessed co-wakers, whether Treat State is chronologically
possible, whether a card moved between the relevant timestamps, and whether a claimed trust relation
is historical or current.

Snuggler still resolve scheduled Starting Role actions. If Trouble or Hunter has a player-bound

Raider, Raider status stays with that player. This rule prevents the pair from creating retroactive
contradictions and makes the Trail of Trouble replay intelligible.

Hunter is acceptable if it protects the Raider. Snuggler's voting doctrine is: vote normally toward
the historical Raider. Consider the secondary Bond-Reveal consequence because catching one bonded
player can expose the other, but do not treat exposure as automatic primary victory unless another
rule also catches the Raider.. Before lock, Guardian may remove one other player from vote-capture
eligibility. The pair must be tested under a clean plurality, a two-way tie, a three-way tie, and a
case where the protected player would otherwise lead. Secondary Bond-Reveal or Hunt Mark effects
occur only after the primary Paw Point catch set is known.

is how that power can change the interpretation of Bandit's evidence. Bandit should respond by
timestamping facts, keeping the Treat State bracket visible, and refusing to let a single role claim
replace the historical-Raider question. In a hostile Bandit state, the best counterplay is usually a
plausible reinterpretation, not an impossible denial of facts another player personally saw.
