INTERACTION 06: BANDIT x HUNTER
-------------------------------

Pair thesis. Hunter can become Bandit-aligned through overlap. This creates a dangerous genuine-
trust story with Guardian and gives hostile side a possible convert whose cover was authentic at
setup. Bandit's anchor is: Starting Bandits know the starting Bandit roster. The earliest starting-
Bandit wake becomes the historical Raider and must steal the Minnow Treats in that window. Other
Bandits are accomplices whose job is to keep the Raider out of the final caught set. Hunter's anchor
is: Hunter and Guardian recognize each other before Hour 1. Hunter wakes twice. On the first wake
that overlaps any starting Bandit’s scheduled wake, Hunter rolls d6 once; 1–2 creates a permanent
player-bound Bandit-side conversion. After Hunter’s later chronological wake, a still-loyal Hunter
places one Hunt Mark. If loyal Hunter is caught in final vote resolution, the mark catches its
target only if that target is Bandit-aligned at resolution. Converted Hunter loses Hunt Mark effect.
The interaction is healthy only if those two mechanics create more interpretable evidence rather
than a hidden exception that players cannot reason about.

the Raider sharply. Hunter failure risk: Hunter can become the game’s most convincing compromised
player because Guardian can truthfully vouch for the starting relationship while lacking conversion
information. Track whether the pair causes automatic clears, automatic guilt, or excessive narrative
branching. The target is constrained ambiguity: two or three plausible stories at the start of
Morning Business, with enough mechanically grounded questions to reduce that set before Paw Point.

Bot behavior. A Bandit bot follows: protect the historical Raider, avoid voting known allies unless
sacrificial distancing materially reduces Raider risk, and prefer frame targets whose public timing
already contains contradictions. A Hunter bot follows: track conversion as a private player-bound
state; never leak it through role-action availability or chat phrasing. Converted bot stops using
loyal Hunt Mark logic.. Neither bot may read the other's hidden state from the authoritative server
merely because the simulation has access to it. Their conversation should be reproducible from
authorized facts plus parsed public claims. This is particularly important in solo play, where
omniscient bots would destroy the deduction game.

QA checklist. Seed at least six pair fixtures: no overlap; ordinary overlap before raid; ordinary
overlap after raid; theft-window overlap; one card movement; one Guardian-protected vote. For each
fixture, save starting cards, Current Cards after every move, d12 schedules, relevant d6 results,
Treat State transitions, player-bound statuses, private fact packets, public claims, Paw Point
drafts, locked votes, protection, catch set, secondary effects, and primary winner. The same event
log must replay to the same result.


INTERACTION 07: BANDIT x GUARDIAN
---------------------------------

Pair thesis. Guardian can accidentally protect Raider, so Bandit benefits from appearing like the
player whose survival is most useful to Business. Guardian pressure is therefore social, not merely
mechanical. Bandit's anchor is: Starting Bandits know the starting Bandit roster. The earliest
starting-Bandit wake becomes the historical Raider and must steal the Minnow Treats in that window.
Other Bandits are accomplices whose job is to keep the Raider out of the final caught set.
Guardian's anchor is: Guardian and Hunter recognize each other before Hour 1. Guardian wakes once
normally. Immediately before Paw Point locks, Guardian privately protects one other player. The
protected player is removed from vote-capture eligibility for that resolution; votes on the
protected player do not determine the top eligible target, so the highest remaining eligible total
is used. Guardian never learns Hunter’s conversion status. The interaction is healthy only if those
two mechanics create more interpretable evidence rather than a hidden exception that players cannot
reason about.

Cheese Thief lens. Treat the pair as two nodes in a wake-time evidence graph. Bandit contributes 1
d12; Guardian contributes 1 d12. Test no-overlap, ordinary overlap, and theft-window overlap
configurations. In each configuration, record who can truthfully report PRESENT, MISSING, co-wakers,
and any state change. The pair should preserve the central before/after puzzle even when role
actions add information or change allegiance.

Among Us lens. Ask which previously reliable tell this pair makes ambiguous. Bandit's special
strength is shared hostile knowledge and the fact that the theft is a historical event that card
movement cannot erase; Guardian's is can reshape the decisive vote and provides one genuine setup
relationship that helps anchor discussion. The design goal is not to make evidence useless. It is to
force the table to add one more question before treating a signal as conclusive. If the pair makes
every observation meaningless, it is too noisy; if one role simply prints the other's guilt, it is
too deterministic.

Information asymmetry. Bandit can directly know own wake hour; starting Bandit roster; Raider
identity; Treat State and co-wakers at the Bandit’s own wake; any later facts gained through card
movement or public discussion. Guardian can directly know starting Hunter identity; own wake
evidence; own chosen protected target; no direct information about Hunter conversion. Neither player
inherits the other's private facts merely because the roles interact. A bot, reconnect path,
spectator, or result preview must enforce the same boundary. Public statements are claims until
independently corroborated or revealed in post-game results.

Claim dynamics. A typical Bandit claim may involve wake hour, Treat State, co-wakers, an ordinary
Business claim, or a carefully chosen special-role claim when plausible; the most durable cover
keeps true chronology and changes only the incriminating implication. A typical Guardian claim may
involve can reveal the original Hunter relationship without revealing intended protection. Guardian
should phrase trust carefully because recognition proves Starting Role, not dawn allegiance.. During
Morning Business, the pair should generate cross-examination rather than forced confession. Useful
questions are whether claimed hours match witnessed co-wakers, whether Treat State is
chronologically possible, whether a card moved between the relevant timestamps, and whether a
claimed trust relation is historical or current.

State-change discipline. If Dooker or Trouble moves either player's Current Card, both Bandit and
Guardian still resolve scheduled Starting Role actions. If Trouble or Hunter has a player-bound
allegiance conversion, that conversion stays with the player. If one of the pair is historical
Raider, Raider status stays with that player. This rule prevents the pair from creating retroactive
contradictions and makes the Trail of Trouble replay intelligible.

Vote pressure. Bandit's voting doctrine is: keep the historical Raider out of every top eligible Paw
Point position, including ties; sacrificing a non-Raider Bandit, converted Trouble, or converted
Hunter is acceptable if it protects the Raider. Guardian's voting doctrine is: Guardian still casts
a normal Paw Point. Protection and vote are separate choices. Protect the player whose survival most
improves the probability of catching Raider, not automatically Hunter.. Before lock, Guardian may
remove one other player from vote-capture eligibility. The pair must be tested under a clean
plurality, a two-way tie, a three-way tie, and a case where the protected player would otherwise
lead. Secondary Bond-Reveal or Hunt Mark effects occur only after the primary Paw Point catch set is
known.

Counterplay for Bandit. The most important risk posed by Guardian is not simply its power text; it
is how that power can change the interpretation of Bandit's evidence. Bandit should respond by
timestamping facts, keeping the Treat State bracket visible, and refusing to let a single role claim
replace the historical-Raider question. In a hostile Bandit state, the best counterplay is usually a
plausible reinterpretation, not an impossible denial of facts another player personally saw.

Counterplay for Guardian. The most important risk posed by Bandit is that its story may be partly
true. Guardian should avoid binary reasoning such as 'one lie means every statement is false' or
'one confirmed fact clears the whole player.' Instead compare independent evidence layers: hour,
Treat State, co-wakers, starting-role capability, Current Card movement, allegiance-changing events,
and voting incentives.

Scenario A: Bandit wakes before Guardian, and the raid occurs between their first relevant
observations. Bandit reports PRESENT; Guardian reports MISSING. This brackets the raid without
proving either role claim. Add one public claim from a third player that conflicts with only one
side. The pair should be able to explain why chronology carries more weight than confidence.

Scenario B: Bandit and Guardian share the theft window. Treat State changes during the window.
Determine whether either role has a conversion trigger, whether the historical Raider is actually
one of them, and which role actions occur after the theft trigger. The rules must make event order
explicit enough that both players can later give internally consistent but strategically different
stories.

Scenario C: a Dooker Trip or Trouble swap changes one participant's Current Card after an earlier
observation. Morning Business now contains a true statement about Starting Role and a different true
statement about current state. The pair passes only if players can reconcile both without treating
one as an automatic lie.

Balance watch. Bandit failure risk: the Treat State timeline can bracket the raid; a witnessed
theft, Dooker Glimpse, Itchy candidate clue, Business die inspection, or contradiction can narrow
the Raider sharply. Guardian failure risk: protection can save the Raider or converted Hunter, can
redirect capture onto an innocent runner-up, and can turn stale trust into a direct hostile
advantage. Track whether the pair causes automatic clears, automatic guilt, or excessive narrative
branching. The target is constrained ambiguity: two or three plausible stories at the start of
Morning Business, with enough mechanically grounded questions to reduce that set before Paw Point.

Bot behavior. A Bandit bot follows: protect the historical Raider, avoid voting known allies unless
sacrificial distancing materially reduces Raider risk, and prefer frame targets whose public timing
already contains contradictions. A Guardian bot follows: evaluate protection after discussion, avoid
self-protection, and model the redirected top-vote outcome rather than merely shielding the most
trusted player. Neither bot may read the other's hidden state from the authoritative server merely
because the simulation has access to it. Their conversation should be reproducible from authorized
facts plus parsed public claims. This is particularly important in solo play, where omniscient bots
would destroy the deduction game.

QA checklist. Seed at least six pair fixtures: no overlap; ordinary overlap before raid; ordinary
overlap after raid; theft-window overlap; one card movement; one Guardian-protected vote. For each
fixture, save starting cards, Current Cards after every move, d12 schedules, relevant d6 results,
Treat State transitions, player-bound statuses, private fact packets, public claims, Paw Point
drafts, locked votes, protection, catch set, secondary effects, and primary winner. The same event
log must replay to the same result.


INTERACTION 08: DOOKER x ITCHY
------------------------------

Pair thesis. Two information roles can overconcentrate certainty. Their outputs should intersect to
narrow narratives without automatically naming the Raider; timestamps and the difference between
allegiance and historical theft matter. Dooker's anchor is: Dooker wakes three times. First
chronological wake is observation. Second chronological wake grants one Glimpse of a chosen player’s
current allegiance as Bandit-aligned or Not Bandit-aligned. Third chronological wake lets Dooker
view one Hammock card and optionally Trip it onto one player, exchanging that player’s Current Card
without seeing the displaced card. Itchy's anchor is: Itchy always receives normal wake evidence. On
d6 5–6, Itchy gains a second distinct wake and, after the raid is committed, receives a partial two-
player clue containing the historical Raider and one valid non-Raider decoy whenever the game can
construct such a pair. The interaction is healthy only if those two mechanics create more
interpretable evidence rather than a hidden exception that players cannot reason about.

Cheese Thief lens. Treat the pair as two nodes in a wake-time evidence graph. Dooker contributes 3
distinct d12s; Itchy contributes 1 d12 normally; 2 distinct d12s on activation. Test no-overlap,
ordinary overlap, and theft-window overlap configurations. In each configuration, record who can
truthfully report PRESENT, MISSING, co-wakers, and any state change. The pair should preserve the
central before/after puzzle even when role actions add information or change allegiance.

Among Us lens. Ask which previously reliable tell this pair makes ambiguous. Dooker's special
strength is broad temporal coverage plus one hard-but-binary scan and one controlled source of card-
state uncertainty; Itchy's is on activated nights, Itchy converts a broad mystery into a constrained
suspect pair while still requiring social deduction. The design goal is not to make evidence
useless. It is to force the table to add one more question before treating a signal as conclusive.
If the pair makes every observation meaningless, it is too noisy; if one role simply prints the
other's guilt, it is too deterministic.

Information asymmetry. Dooker can directly know three wake windows, three Treat States/co-waker
sets, one binary allegiance result at a specific moment, one known Hammock card, and exact knowledge
of whether/where the viewed Hammock card was moved. Itchy can directly know activation result; one
or two wake windows; Treat State/co-wakers; if activated, exactly two clue candidates with the
guarantee that one is the historical Raider when a valid decoy exists. Neither player inherits the
other's private facts merely because the roles interact. A bot, reconnect path, spectator, or result
preview must enforce the same boundary. Public statements are claims until independently
corroborated or revealed in post-game results.

Claim dynamics. A typical Dooker claim may involve can reveal all three hours or stagger
information, can disclose Glimpse without immediately revealing which wake produced it, and can
truthfully describe the viewed Hammock card while remaining uncertain about the displaced card. A
typical Itchy claim may involve can truthfully report no activation, can reveal one or both wake
windows, and can reveal candidate pair without claiming certainty about which candidate is Raider.
During Morning Business, the pair should generate cross-examination rather than forced confession.
Useful questions are whether claimed hours match witnessed co-wakers, whether Treat State is
chronologically possible, whether a card moved between the relevant timestamps, and whether a
claimed trust relation is historical or current.

State-change discipline. If Dooker or Trouble moves either player's Current Card, both Dooker and
Itchy still resolve scheduled Starting Role actions. If Trouble or Hunter has a player-bound
allegiance conversion, that conversion stays with the player. If one of the pair is historical
Raider, Raider status stays with that player. This rule prevents the pair from creating retroactive
contradictions and makes the Trail of Trouble replay intelligible.

Vote pressure. Dooker's voting doctrine is: vote for the player whose total story best fits the
Raider timeline after accounting for stale Glimpse information and card movement; do not
mechanically vote every Bandit-aligned Glimpse target if the historical Raider evidence points
elsewhere. Itchy's voting doctrine is: use the clue to constrain rather than dictate. Prefer the
candidate whose wake story conflicts with the public theft bracket, but remain willing to vote
outside the pair only if a rules edge case invalidated the normal guarantee. Before lock, Guardian
may remove one other player from vote-capture eligibility. The pair must be tested under a clean
plurality, a two-way tie, a three-way tie, and a case where the protected player would otherwise
lead. Secondary Bond-Reveal or Hunt Mark effects occur only after the primary Paw Point catch set is
known.

Counterplay for Dooker. The most important risk posed by Itchy is not simply its power text; it is
how that power can change the interpretation of Dooker's evidence. Dooker should respond by
timestamping facts, keeping the Treat State bracket visible, and refusing to let a single role claim
replace the historical-Raider question. In a hostile Dooker state, the best counterplay is usually a
plausible reinterpretation, not an impossible denial of facts another player personally saw.

Counterplay for Itchy. The most important risk posed by Dooker is that its story may be partly true.
Itchy should avoid binary reasoning such as 'one lie means every statement is false' or 'one
confirmed fact clears the whole player.' Instead compare independent evidence layers: hour, Treat
State, co-wakers, starting-role capability, Current Card movement, allegiance-changing events, and
voting incentives.

Scenario A: Dooker wakes before Itchy, and the raid occurs between their first relevant
observations. Dooker reports PRESENT; Itchy reports MISSING. This brackets the raid without proving
either role claim. Add one public claim from a third player that conflicts with only one side. The
pair should be able to explain why chronology carries more weight than confidence.

Scenario B: Dooker and Itchy share the theft window. Treat State changes during the window.
Determine whether either role has a conversion trigger, whether the historical Raider is actually
one of them, and which role actions occur after the theft trigger. The rules must make event order
explicit enough that both players can later give internally consistent but strategically different
stories.

Scenario C: a Dooker Trip or Trouble swap changes one participant's Current Card after an earlier
observation. Morning Business now contains a true statement about Starting Role and a different true
statement about current state. The pair passes only if players can reconcile both without treating
one as an automatic lie.

Balance watch. Dooker failure risk: three wakes make Dooker easier to cross-check; the Trip can
accidentally help Bandits, can make Dooker’s earlier Glimpse stale, and can create false confidence
if players conflate current allegiance with historical Raider identity. Itchy failure risk:
activation is uncommon, candidate evidence can be framed, and Itchy becomes an attractive Bandit
target for contradiction or false counterclaims because the clue is powerful but not self-verifying.
Track whether the pair causes automatic clears, automatic guilt, or excessive narrative branching.
The target is constrained ambiguity: two or three plausible stories at the start of Morning
Business, with enough mechanically grounded questions to reduce that set before Paw Point.

Bot behavior. A Dooker bot follows: separate historical theft suspicion from current-allegiance
suspicion, preserve timestamps on Glimpse/Trip, and never invent knowledge of the card displaced by
Trip. A Itchy bot follows: never turn a two-candidate clue into an exact accusation; record which
evidence is ordinary wake evidence and which evidence comes from activation. Neither bot may read
the other's hidden state from the authoritative server merely because the simulation has access to
it. Their conversation should be reproducible from authorized facts plus parsed public claims. This
is particularly important in solo play, where omniscient bots would destroy the deduction game.

QA checklist. Seed at least six pair fixtures: no overlap; ordinary overlap before raid; ordinary
overlap after raid; theft-window overlap; one card movement; one Guardian-protected vote. For each
fixture, save starting cards, Current Cards after every move, d12 schedules, relevant d6 results,
Treat State transitions, player-bound statuses, private fact packets, public claims, Paw Point
drafts, locked votes, protection, catch set, secondary effects, and primary winner. The same event
log must replay to the same result.


INTERACTION 09: DOOKER x TROUBLE
--------------------------------

Pair thesis. Dooker can move a known Hammock card while Trouble moves two unknown player cards.
Together they make Current Card highly fluid, so starting-role actions and historical statuses must
remain separate. Dooker's anchor is: Dooker wakes three times. First chronological wake is
observation. Second chronological wake grants one Glimpse of a chosen player’s current allegiance as
Bandit-aligned or Not Bandit-aligned. Third chronological wake lets Dooker view one Hammock card and
optionally Trip it onto one player, exchanging that player’s Current Card without seeing the
displaced card. Trouble's anchor is: At Trouble’s wake, Trouble blindly swaps the Current Cards of
two distinct other players. If Trouble is awake in the exact theft window, Trouble becomes In On It
before the swap resolves and receives the historical Raider identity as part of that conversion. The
interaction is healthy only if those two mechanics create more interpretable evidence rather than a
hidden exception that players cannot reason about.

Cheese Thief lens. Treat the pair as two nodes in a wake-time evidence graph. Dooker contributes 3

strength is broad temporal coverage plus one hard-but-binary scan and one controlled source of card-

collision, can become a thematically clean Follower-like accomplice without adding a ninth role. The

the fact of conversion and historical Raider identity. Neither player inherits the other's private

change; a loyal Trouble can use swap facts to explain why role claims may no longer match Current

INTERACTION 10: DOOKER x BUSINESS
---------------------------------

Pair thesis. Business provides broad ordinary corroboration; Dooker provides deeper but narrower
special facts. Healthy play has Business test Dooker’s chronology rather than simply defer to a
power role. Dooker's anchor is: Dooker wakes three times. First chronological wake is observation.
Second chronological wake grants one Glimpse of a chosen player’s current allegiance as Bandit-
aligned or Not Bandit-aligned. Third chronological wake lets Dooker view one Hammock card and
optionally Trip it onto one player, exchanging that player’s Current Card without seeing the
displaced card. Business's anchor is: Business is the ordinary evidence role. It sees Treat State
and co-wakers at its single wake. If it wakes alone, it may inspect exactly one wake result
belonging to one chosen other player; for a multi-wake target, the app reveals one randomly selected
result and does not reveal how many additional wake results the target has. The interaction is
healthy only if those two mechanics create more interpretable evidence rather than a hidden
exception that players cannot reason about.

Cheese Thief lens. Treat the pair as two nodes in a wake-time evidence graph. Dooker contributes 3

little privileged power, can be impersonated easily, and may overvalue a single inspected die when
the target is a multi-wake role. Track whether the pair causes automatic clears, automatic guilt, or
excessive narrative branching. The target is constrained ambiguity: two or three plausible stories
at the start of Morning Business, with enough mechanically grounded questions to reduce that set
before Paw Point.

Bot behavior. A Dooker bot follows: separate historical theft suspicion from current-allegiance
suspicion, preserve timestamps on Glimpse/Trip, and never invent knowledge of the card displaced by
Trip. A Business bot follows: prioritize timeline consistency, ask direct questions about wake hours
and Treat State, and treat inspected multi-wake results as partial rather than exhaustive. Neither
bot may read the other's hidden state from the authoritative server merely because the simulation
has access to it. Their conversation should be reproducible from authorized facts plus parsed public
claims. This is particularly important in solo play, where omniscient bots would destroy the
deduction game.

QA checklist. Seed at least six pair fixtures: no overlap; ordinary overlap before raid; ordinary
overlap after raid; theft-window overlap; one card movement; one Guardian-protected vote. For each
fixture, save starting cards, Current Cards after every move, d12 schedules, relevant d6 results,
Treat State transitions, player-bound statuses, private fact packets, public claims, Paw Point
drafts, locked votes, protection, catch set, secondary effects, and primary winner. The same event
log must replay to the same result.


INTERACTION 11: DOOKER x SNUGGLER
---------------------------------

Pair thesis. Dooker’s information can tempt players to treat bonded relationships as alignment
clusters. The correct model keeps bond, current allegiance, and historical Raider identity distinct.
Dooker's anchor is: Dooker wakes three times. First chronological wake is observation. Second
chronological wake grants one Glimpse of a chosen player’s current allegiance as Bandit-aligned or
Not Bandit-aligned. Third chronological wake lets Dooker view one Hammock card and optionally Trip
it onto one player, exchanging that player’s Current Card without seeing the displaced card.
Snuggler's anchor is: Before Hour 1, Snuggler chooses two distinct other players to become Snuggle-
Bonded. The bonded players learn each other’s identity as partners but not each other’s roles. Bond
status is player-bound. If one bonded player is caught by vote or a valid Hunt Mark, the other
becomes Bond-Revealed in the final resolution; Bond-Reveal exposes identity/current card for results
but is not itself a Paw Point capture. The interaction is healthy only if those two mechanics create
more interpretable evidence rather than a hidden exception that players cannot reason about.

Cheese Thief lens. Treat the pair as two nodes in a wake-time evidence graph. Dooker contributes 3
distinct d12s; Snuggler contributes 1 d12. Test no-overlap, ordinary overlap, and theft-window
overlap configurations. In each configuration, record who can truthfully report PRESENT, MISSING,
co-wakers, and any state change. The pair should preserve the central before/after puzzle even when
role actions add information or change allegiance.

Among Us lens. Ask which previously reliable tell this pair makes ambiguous. Dooker's special
strength is broad temporal coverage plus one hard-but-binary scan and one controlled source of card-
state uncertainty; Snuggler's is creates a human trust channel that is mechanically real but
alignment-neutral, making social alliances less diagnostic and producing useful ambiguity without
adding another hostile faction. The design goal is not to make evidence useless. It is to force the
table to add one more question before treating a signal as conclusive. If the pair makes every
observation meaningless, it is too noisy; if one role simply prints the other's guilt, it is too
deterministic.

Information asymmetry. Dooker can directly know three wake windows, three Treat States/co-waker
sets, one binary allegiance result at a specific moment, one known Hammock card, and exact knowledge
of whether/where the viewed Hammock card was moved. Snuggler can directly know the selected bonded
pair; own normal wake evidence; no alignment information about the pair unless obtained elsewhere.
Neither player inherits the other's private facts merely because the roles interact. A bot,
reconnect path, spectator, or result preview must enforce the same boundary. Public statements are
claims until independently corroborated or revealed in post-game results.

Claim dynamics. A typical Dooker claim may involve can reveal all three hours or stagger
information, can disclose Glimpse without immediately revealing which wake produced it, and can
truthfully describe the viewed Hammock card while remaining uncertain about the displaced card. A
typical Snuggler claim may involve can reveal or conceal the pair. Bonded players may explain
cooperation by claiming the bond, but a bond claim never clears either player because one or both
may be hostile. During Morning Business, the pair should generate cross-examination rather than
forced confession. Useful questions are whether claimed hours match witnessed co-wakers, whether
Treat State is chronologically possible, whether a card moved between the relevant timestamps, and
whether a claimed trust relation is historical or current.

State-change discipline. If Dooker or Trouble moves either player's Current Card, both Dooker and
Snuggler still resolve scheduled Starting Role actions. If Trouble or Hunter has a player-bound
allegiance conversion, that conversion stays with the player. If one of the pair is historical
Raider, Raider status stays with that player. This rule prevents the pair from creating retroactive
contradictions and makes the Trail of Trouble replay intelligible.

Vote pressure. Dooker's voting doctrine is: vote for the player whose total story best fits the
Raider timeline after accounting for stale Glimpse information and card movement; do not
mechanically vote every Bandit-aligned Glimpse target if the historical Raider evidence points
elsewhere. Snuggler's voting doctrine is: vote normally toward the historical Raider. Consider the
secondary Bond-Reveal consequence because catching one bonded player can expose the other, but do
not treat exposure as automatic primary victory unless another rule also catches the Raider.. Before
lock, Guardian may remove one other player from vote-capture eligibility. The pair must be tested
under a clean plurality, a two-way tie, a three-way tie, and a case where the protected player would
otherwise lead. Secondary Bond-Reveal or Hunt Mark effects occur only after the primary Paw Point
catch set is known.

Counterplay for Dooker. The most important risk posed by Snuggler is not simply its power text; it
is how that power can change the interpretation of Dooker's evidence. Dooker should respond by
timestamping facts, keeping the Treat State bracket visible, and refusing to let a single role claim
replace the historical-Raider question. In a hostile Dooker state, the best counterplay is usually a
plausible reinterpretation, not an impossible denial of facts another player personally saw.

Counterplay for Snuggler. The most important risk posed by Dooker is that its story may be partly
true. Snuggler should avoid binary reasoning such as 'one lie means every statement is false' or
'one confirmed fact clears the whole player.' Instead compare independent evidence layers: hour,
Treat State, co-wakers, starting-role capability, Current Card movement, allegiance-changing events,
and voting incentives.

Scenario A: Dooker wakes before Snuggler, and the raid occurs between their first relevant
observations. Dooker reports PRESENT; Snuggler reports MISSING. This brackets the raid without
proving either role claim. Add one public claim from a third player that conflicts with only one
side. The pair should be able to explain why chronology carries more weight than confidence.

Scenario B: Dooker and Snuggler share the theft window. Treat State changes during the window.
Determine whether either role has a conversion trigger, whether the historical Raider is actually
one of them, and which role actions occur after the theft trigger. The rules must make event order
explicit enough that both players can later give internally consistent but strategically different
stories.

Scenario C: a Dooker Trip or Trouble swap changes one participant's Current Card after an earlier
observation. Morning Business now contains a true statement about Starting Role and a different true
statement about current state. The pair passes only if players can reconcile both without treating
one as an automatic lie.

Balance watch. Dooker failure risk: three wakes make Dooker easier to cross-check; the Trip can
accidentally help Bandits, can make Dooker’s earlier Glimpse stale, and can create false confidence
if players conflate current allegiance with historical Raider identity. Snuggler failure risk: a
bond can connect Raider to a Business player, connect two Business players who then overtrust each
other, or become strategically irrelevant if neither bonded player is pressured. Track whether the
pair causes automatic clears, automatic guilt, or excessive narrative branching. The target is
constrained ambiguity: two or three plausible stories at the start of Morning Business, with enough
mechanically grounded questions to reduce that set before Paw Point.

Bot behavior. A Dooker bot follows: separate historical theft suspicion from current-allegiance
suspicion, preserve timestamps on Glimpse/Trip, and never invent knowledge of the card displaced by
Trip. A Snuggler bot follows: use the bond as relationship evidence only; never assign negative or
positive alignment weight merely because players are bonded. Neither bot may read the other's hidden
state from the authoritative server merely because the simulation has access to it. Their
conversation should be reproducible from authorized facts plus parsed public claims. This is
particularly important in solo play, where omniscient bots would destroy the deduction game.

QA checklist. Seed at least six pair fixtures: no overlap; ordinary overlap before raid; ordinary
overlap after raid; theft-window overlap; one card movement; one Guardian-protected vote. For each
fixture, save starting cards, Current Cards after every move, d12 schedules, relevant d6 results,
Treat State transitions, player-bound statuses, private fact packets, public claims, Paw Point
drafts, locked votes, protection, catch set, secondary effects, and primary winner. The same event
log must replay to the same result.


INTERACTION 12: DOOKER x HUNTER
-------------------------------

Pair thesis. A Glimpse after Hunter conversion may truthfully say Bandit-aligned while Guardian
still truthfully says Hunter was recognized at setup. This is a designed contradiction in inference,
not in facts. Dooker's anchor is: Dooker wakes three times. First chronological wake is observation.
Second chronological wake grants one Glimpse of a chosen player’s current allegiance as Bandit-
aligned or Not Bandit-aligned. Third chronological wake lets Dooker view one Hammock card and
optionally Trip it onto one player, exchanging that player’s Current Card without seeing the
displaced card. Hunter's anchor is: Hunter and Guardian recognize each other before Hour 1. Hunter
wakes twice. On the first wake that overlaps any starting Bandit’s scheduled wake, Hunter rolls d6
once; 1–2 creates a permanent player-bound Bandit-side conversion. After Hunter’s later
chronological wake, a still-loyal Hunter places one Hunt Mark. If loyal Hunter is caught in final
vote resolution, the mark catches its target only if that target is Bandit-aligned at resolution.
Converted Hunter loses Hunt Mark effect. The interaction is healthy only if those two mechanics
create more interpretable evidence rather than a hidden exception that players cannot reason about.

Cheese Thief lens. Treat the pair as two nodes in a wake-time evidence graph. Dooker contributes 3
distinct d12s; Hunter contributes 2 distinct d12s. Test no-overlap, ordinary overlap, and theft-
window overlap configurations. In each configuration, record who can truthfully report PRESENT,
MISSING, co-wakers, and any state change. The pair should preserve the central before/after puzzle
even when role actions add information or change allegiance.

This is deliberately dangerous because a once-valid trust relationship can become stale.. During

INTERACTION 13: DOOKER x GUARDIAN
---------------------------------

Pair thesis. Guardian may protect someone Dooker has scanned; the table must decide whether a
timestamped Glimpse is still current after later movement or conversion before spending a vote
shield. Dooker's anchor is: Dooker wakes three times. First chronological wake is observation.
Second chronological wake grants one Glimpse of a chosen player’s current allegiance as Bandit-
aligned or Not Bandit-aligned. Third chronological wake lets Dooker view one Hammock card and
optionally Trip it onto one player, exchanging that player’s Current Card without seeing the
displaced card. Guardian's anchor is: Guardian and Hunter recognize each other before Hour 1.
Guardian wakes once normally. Immediately before Paw Point locks, Guardian privately protects one
other player. The protected player is removed from vote-capture eligibility for that resolution;
votes on the protected player do not determine the top eligible target, so the highest remaining
eligible total is used. Guardian never learns Hunter’s conversion status. The interaction is healthy
only if those two mechanics create more interpretable evidence rather than a hidden exception that
players cannot reason about.

relationship that helps anchor discussion. The design goal is not to make evidence useless. It is to

catching Raider, not automatically Hunter.. Before lock, Guardian may remove one other player from

INTERACTION 14: ITCHY x TROUBLE
-------------------------------

Pair thesis. Trouble can be one of Itchy’s candidates yet may or may not be Raider; conversion does
not turn Trouble into the historical thief. The pair teaches suspect-list discipline. Itchy's anchor
is: Itchy always receives normal wake evidence. On d6 5–6, Itchy gains a second distinct wake and,
after the raid is committed, receives a partial two-player clue containing the historical Raider and
one valid non-Raider decoy whenever the game can construct such a pair. Trouble's anchor is: At
Trouble’s wake, Trouble blindly swaps the Current Cards of two distinct other players. If Trouble is
awake in the exact theft window, Trouble becomes In On It before the swap resolves and receives the
historical Raider identity as part of that conversion. The interaction is healthy only if those two
mechanics create more interpretable evidence rather than a hidden exception that players cannot
reason about.

Cheese Thief lens. Treat the pair as two nodes in a wake-time evidence graph. Itchy contributes 1
d12 normally; 2 distinct d12s on activation; Trouble contributes 1 d12. Test no-overlap, ordinary
overlap, and theft-window overlap configurations. In each configuration, record who can truthfully
report PRESENT, MISSING, co-wakers, and any state change. The pair should preserve the central
before/after puzzle even when role actions add information or change allegiance.

Among Us lens. Ask which previously reliable tell this pair makes ambiguous. Itchy's special
strength is on activated nights, Itchy converts a broad mystery into a constrained suspect pair
while still requiring social deduction; Trouble's is creates legitimate card-state ambiguity and, on
theft-window collision, can become a thematically clean Follower-like accomplice without adding a
ninth role. The design goal is not to make evidence useless. It is to force the table to add one
more question before treating a signal as conclusive. If the pair makes every observation
meaningless, it is too noisy; if one role simply prints the other's guilt, it is too deterministic.

Information asymmetry. Itchy can directly know activation result; one or two wake windows; Treat
State/co-wakers; if activated, exactly two clue candidates with the guarantee that one is the
historical Raider when a valid decoy exists. Trouble can directly know own wake evidence; exact
identities of the two positions swapped; no knowledge of either swapped card; if converted, the fact
of conversion and historical Raider identity. Neither player inherits the other's private facts
merely because the roles interact. A bot, reconnect path, spectator, or result preview must enforce
the same boundary. Public statements are claims until independently corroborated or revealed in
post-game results.

Claim dynamics. A typical Itchy claim may involve can truthfully report no activation, can reveal
one or both wake windows, and can reveal candidate pair without claiming certainty about which
candidate is Raider. A typical Trouble claim may involve can truthfully reveal the swap while
withholding allegiance change; a loyal Trouble can use swap facts to explain why role claims may no
longer match Current Cards; a converted Trouble can exploit the same uncertainty to shield the
Raider. During Morning Business, the pair should generate cross-examination rather than forced
confession. Useful questions are whether claimed hours match witnessed co-wakers, whether Treat
State is chronologically possible, whether a card moved between the relevant timestamps, and whether
a claimed trust relation is historical or current.

State-change discipline. If Dooker or Trouble moves either player's Current Card, both Itchy and
Trouble still resolve scheduled Starting Role actions. If Trouble or Hunter has a player-bound
allegiance conversion, that conversion stays with the player. If one of the pair is historical
Raider, Raider status stays with that player. This rule prevents the pair from creating retroactive
contradictions and makes the Trail of Trouble replay intelligible.

Vote pressure. Itchy's voting doctrine is: use the clue to constrain rather than dictate. Prefer the
candidate whose wake story conflicts with the public theft bracket, but remain willing to vote
outside the pair only if a rules edge case invalidated the normal guarantee. Trouble's voting
doctrine is: loyal Trouble votes for the historical Raider; converted Trouble protects the
historical Raider even if that means accepting personal suspicion. Trouble’s own capture never
substitutes for capturing the Raider.. Before lock, Guardian may remove one other player from vote-
capture eligibility. The pair must be tested under a clean plurality, a two-way tie, a three-way
tie, and a case where the protected player would otherwise lead. Secondary Bond-Reveal or Hunt Mark
effects occur only after the primary Paw Point catch set is known.

Counterplay for Itchy. The most important risk posed by Trouble is not simply its power text; it is
how that power can change the interpretation of Itchy's evidence. Itchy should respond by
timestamping facts, keeping the Treat State bracket visible, and refusing to let a single role claim
replace the historical-Raider question. In a hostile Itchy state, the best counterplay is usually a
plausible reinterpretation, not an impossible denial of facts another player personally saw.

Counterplay for Trouble. The most important risk posed by Itchy is that its story may be partly
true. Trouble should avoid binary reasoning such as 'one lie means every statement is false' or 'one
confirmed fact clears the whole player.' Instead compare independent evidence layers: hour, Treat
State, co-wakers, starting-role capability, Current Card movement, allegiance-changing events, and
voting incentives.

Scenario A: Itchy wakes before Trouble, and the raid occurs between their first relevant
observations. Itchy reports PRESENT; Trouble reports MISSING. This brackets the raid without proving
either role claim. Add one public claim from a third player that conflicts with only one side. The
pair should be able to explain why chronology carries more weight than confidence.

Scenario B: Itchy and Trouble share the theft window. Treat State changes during the window.
Determine whether either role has a conversion trigger, whether the historical Raider is actually
one of them, and which role actions occur after the theft trigger. The rules must make event order
explicit enough that both players can later give internally consistent but strategically different
stories.

Scenario C: a Dooker Trip or Trouble swap changes one participant's Current Card after an earlier
observation. Morning Business now contains a true statement about Starting Role and a different true
statement about current state. The pair passes only if players can reconcile both without treating
one as an automatic lie.

Balance watch. Itchy failure risk: activation is uncommon, candidate evidence can be framed, and
Itchy becomes an attractive Bandit target for contradiction or false counterclaims because the clue
is powerful but not self-verifying. Trouble failure risk: blindness is real; Trouble can
accidentally move a Bandit card, disrupt a useful Business role, or produce a swap that later makes
Trouble look responsible for contradictions it could not predict. Track whether the pair causes
automatic clears, automatic guilt, or excessive narrative branching. The target is constrained
ambiguity: two or three plausible stories at the start of Morning Business, with enough mechanically
grounded questions to reduce that set before Paw Point.

Bot behavior. A Itchy bot follows: never turn a two-candidate clue into an exact accusation; record
which evidence is ordinary wake evidence and which evidence comes from activation. A Trouble bot
follows: maintain a strict distinction between swap-position knowledge and card-identity ignorance.
If converted, the bot may coordinate strategically from known Raider identity but cannot fabricate
having seen the swapped cards.. Neither bot may read the other's hidden state from the authoritative
server merely because the simulation has access to it. Their conversation should be reproducible
from authorized facts plus parsed public claims. This is particularly important in solo play, where
omniscient bots would destroy the deduction game.

QA checklist. Seed at least six pair fixtures: no overlap; ordinary overlap before raid; ordinary
overlap after raid; theft-window overlap; one card movement; one Guardian-protected vote. For each
fixture, save starting cards, Current Cards after every move, d12 schedules, relevant d6 results,
Treat State transitions, player-bound statuses, private fact packets, public claims, Paw Point
drafts, locked votes, protection, catch set, secondary effects, and primary winner. The same event
log must replay to the same result.


INTERACTION 15: ITCHY x BUSINESS
--------------------------------

Pair thesis. Business timeline evidence is the best way to discriminate between Itchy’s two
candidates. The clue narrows; ordinary evidence decides. Itchy's anchor is: Itchy always receives
normal wake evidence. On d6 5–6, Itchy gains a second distinct wake and, after the raid is
committed, receives a partial two-player clue containing the historical Raider and one valid non-
Raider decoy whenever the game can construct such a pair. Business's anchor is: Business is the
ordinary evidence role. It sees Treat State and co-wakers at its single wake. If it wakes alone, it
may inspect exactly one wake result belonging to one chosen other player; for a multi-wake target,
the app reveals one randomly selected result and does not reveal how many additional wake results
the target has. The interaction is healthy only if those two mechanics create more interpretable
evidence rather than a hidden exception that players cannot reason about.

Cheese Thief lens. Treat the pair as two nodes in a wake-time evidence graph. Itchy contributes 1
d12 normally; 2 distinct d12s on activation; Business contributes 1 d12. Test no-overlap, ordinary
overlap, and theft-window overlap configurations. In each configuration, record who can truthfully
report PRESENT, MISSING, co-wakers, and any state change. The pair should preserve the central
before/after puzzle even when role actions add information or change allegiance.

Among Us lens. Ask which previously reliable tell this pair makes ambiguous. Itchy's special
strength is on activated nights, Itchy converts a broad mystery into a constrained suspect pair
while still requiring social deduction; Business's is forms the baseline evidence network against
which special-role claims and hostile stories can be tested. The design goal is not to make evidence
useless. It is to force the table to add one more question before treating a signal as conclusive.
If the pair makes every observation meaningless, it is too noisy; if one role simply prints the
other's guilt, it is too deterministic.

Information asymmetry. Itchy can directly know activation result; one or two wake windows; Treat
State/co-wakers; if activated, exactly two clue candidates with the guarantee that one is the
historical Raider when a valid decoy exists. Business can directly know own wake hour; Treat State;
co-wakers; when alone, one inspected wake result from a chosen player; no direct role scan and no
allegiance scan. Neither player inherits the other's private facts merely because the roles
interact. A bot, reconnect path, spectator, or result preview must enforce the same boundary. Public
statements are claims until independently corroborated or revealed in post-game results.

Claim dynamics. A typical Itchy claim may involve can truthfully report no activation, can reveal
one or both wake windows, and can reveal candidate pair without claiming certainty about which
candidate is Raider. A typical Business claim may involve simple claims about hour, Treat State, co-
wakers, and optional inspected result. Because Bandits can imitate this structure, credibility must
come from consistency and corroboration rather than uniqueness. During Morning Business, the pair
should generate cross-examination rather than forced confession. Useful questions are whether
claimed hours match witnessed co-wakers, whether Treat State is chronologically possible, whether a
card moved between the relevant timestamps, and whether a claimed trust relation is historical or
current.

State-change discipline. If Dooker or Trouble moves either player's Current Card, both Itchy and
Business still resolve scheduled Starting Role actions. If Trouble or Hunter has a player-bound
allegiance conversion, that conversion stays with the player. If one of the pair is historical
Raider, Raider status stays with that player. This rule prevents the pair from creating retroactive
contradictions and makes the Trail of Trouble replay intelligible.

Vote pressure. Itchy's voting doctrine is: use the clue to constrain rather than dictate. Prefer the
candidate whose wake story conflicts with the public theft bracket, but remain willing to vote
outside the pair only if a rules edge case invalidated the normal guarantee. Business's voting
doctrine is: vote from the reconstructed theft bracket and contradiction network, not from role
glamour. A plain Business claim that fits two other observations can be stronger than a flashy
special-role claim that cannot be reconciled.. Before lock, Guardian may remove one other player
from vote-capture eligibility. The pair must be tested under a clean plurality, a two-way tie, a
three-way tie, and a case where the protected player would otherwise lead. Secondary Bond-Reveal or
Hunt Mark effects occur only after the primary Paw Point catch set is known.

Counterplay for Itchy. The most important risk posed by Business is not simply its power text; it is
how that power can change the interpretation of Itchy's evidence. Itchy should respond by
timestamping facts, keeping the Treat State bracket visible, and refusing to let a single role claim
replace the historical-Raider question. In a hostile Itchy state, the best counterplay is usually a
plausible reinterpretation, not an impossible denial of facts another player personally saw.

Counterplay for Business. The most important risk posed by Itchy is that its story may be partly
true. Business should avoid binary reasoning such as 'one lie means every statement is false' or
'one confirmed fact clears the whole player.' Instead compare independent evidence layers: hour,
Treat State, co-wakers, starting-role capability, Current Card movement, allegiance-changing events,
and voting incentives.

Scenario A: Itchy wakes before Business, and the raid occurs between their first relevant
observations. Itchy reports PRESENT; Business reports MISSING. This brackets the raid without
proving either role claim. Add one public claim from a third player that conflicts with only one
side. The pair should be able to explain why chronology carries more weight than confidence.

Scenario B: Itchy and Business share the theft window. Treat State changes during the window.
Determine whether either role has a conversion trigger, whether the historical Raider is actually
one of them, and which role actions occur after the theft trigger. The rules must make event order
explicit enough that both players can later give internally consistent but strategically different
stories.

Scenario C: a Dooker Trip or Trouble swap changes one participant's Current Card after an earlier
observation. Morning Business now contains a true statement about Starting Role and a different true
statement about current state. The pair passes only if players can reconcile both without treating
one as an automatic lie.

Balance watch. Itchy failure risk: activation is uncommon, candidate evidence can be framed, and
Itchy becomes an attractive Bandit target for contradiction or false counterclaims because the clue
is powerful but not self-verifying. Business failure risk: has little privileged power, can be
impersonated easily, and may overvalue a single inspected die when the target is a multi-wake role.
Track whether the pair causes automatic clears, automatic guilt, or excessive narrative branching.
The target is constrained ambiguity: two or three plausible stories at the start of Morning
Business, with enough mechanically grounded questions to reduce that set before Paw Point.

Bot behavior. A Itchy bot follows: never turn a two-candidate clue into an exact accusation; record
which evidence is ordinary wake evidence and which evidence comes from activation. A Business bot
follows: prioritize timeline consistency, ask direct questions about wake hours and Treat State, and
treat inspected multi-wake results as partial rather than exhaustive. Neither bot may read the
other's hidden state from the authoritative server merely because the simulation has access to it.
Their conversation should be reproducible from authorized facts plus parsed public claims. This is
particularly important in solo play, where omniscient bots would destroy the deduction game.

QA checklist. Seed at least six pair fixtures: no overlap; ordinary overlap before raid; ordinary
overlap after raid; theft-window overlap; one card movement; one Guardian-protected vote. For each
fixture, save starting cards, Current Cards after every move, d12 schedules, relevant d6 results,
Treat State transitions, player-bound statuses, private fact packets, public claims, Paw Point
drafts, locked votes, protection, catch set, secondary effects, and primary winner. The same event
log must replay to the same result.


INTERACTION 16: ITCHY x SNUGGLER
--------------------------------

Pair thesis. A candidate may be bonded to another player, creating pressure to overread cooperation.
Bond is socially relevant but cannot clear either candidate. Itchy's anchor is: Itchy always
receives normal wake evidence. On d6 5–6, Itchy gains a second distinct wake and, after the raid is
committed, receives a partial two-player clue containing the historical Raider and one valid non-
Raider decoy whenever the game can construct such a pair. Snuggler's anchor is: Before Hour 1,
Snuggler chooses two distinct other players to become Snuggle-Bonded. The bonded players learn each
other’s identity as partners but not each other’s roles. Bond status is player-bound. If one bonded
player is caught by vote or a valid Hunt Mark, the other becomes Bond-Revealed in the final
resolution; Bond-Reveal exposes identity/current card for results but is not itself a Paw Point
capture. The interaction is healthy only if those two mechanics create more interpretable evidence
rather than a hidden exception that players cannot reason about.

Cheese Thief lens. Treat the pair as two nodes in a wake-time evidence graph. Itchy contributes 1
d12 normally; 2 distinct d12s on activation; Snuggler contributes 1 d12. Test no-overlap, ordinary
overlap, and theft-window overlap configurations. In each configuration, record who can truthfully
report PRESENT, MISSING, co-wakers, and any state change. The pair should preserve the central
before/after puzzle even when role actions add information or change allegiance.

useful ambiguity without adding another hostile faction. The design goal is not to make evidence
useless. It is to force the table to add one more question before treating a signal as conclusive.

one or both wake windows, and can reveal candidate pair without claiming certainty about which

players may explain cooperation by claiming the bond, but a bond claim never clears either player

examination rather than forced confession. Useful questions are whether claimed hours match
witnessed co-wakers, whether Treat State is chronologically possible, whether a card moved between
the relevant timestamps, and whether a claimed trust relation is historical or current.

INTERACTION 17: ITCHY x HUNTER
------------------------------

Pair thesis. If Hunter converts, Itchy’s Raider clue still points to the historical Raider rather
than the convert. This cleanly separates “hostile now” from “committed the theft.” Itchy's anchor
is: Itchy always receives normal wake evidence. On d6 5–6, Itchy gains a second distinct wake and,
after the raid is committed, receives a partial two-player clue containing the historical Raider and
one valid non-Raider decoy whenever the game can construct such a pair. Hunter's anchor is: Hunter
and Guardian recognize each other before Hour 1. Hunter wakes twice. On the first wake that overlaps
any starting Bandit’s scheduled wake, Hunter rolls d6 once; 1–2 creates a permanent player-bound
Bandit-side conversion. After Hunter’s later chronological wake, a still-loyal Hunter places one
Hunt Mark. If loyal Hunter is caught in final vote resolution, the mark catches its target only if
that target is Bandit-aligned at resolution. Converted Hunter loses Hunt Mark effect. The
interaction is healthy only if those two mechanics create more interpretable evidence rather than a
hidden exception that players cannot reason about.

Cheese Thief lens. Treat the pair as two nodes in a wake-time evidence graph. Itchy contributes 1

INTERACTION 18: ITCHY x GUARDIAN
--------------------------------

conversation should be reproducible from authorized facts plus parsed public claims. This is
particularly important in solo play, where omniscient bots would destroy the deduction game.

QA checklist. Seed at least six pair fixtures: no overlap; ordinary overlap before raid; ordinary
overlap after raid; theft-window overlap; one card movement; one Guardian-protected vote. For each
fixture, save starting cards, Current Cards after every move, d12 schedules, relevant d6 results,
Treat State transitions, player-bound statuses, private fact packets, public claims, Paw Point
drafts, locked votes, protection, catch set, secondary effects, and primary winner. The same event
log must replay to the same result.


INTERACTION 19: TROUBLE x BUSINESS
----------------------------------

Pair thesis. Trouble’s disclosed swap explains why a Business claim about starting role can coexist
with a different Current Card. Business evidence remains about what was observed, not what card sits
there at dawn. Trouble's anchor is: At Trouble’s wake, Trouble blindly swaps the Current Cards of
two distinct other players. If Trouble is awake in the exact theft window, Trouble becomes In On It
before the swap resolves and receives the historical Raider identity as part of that conversion.
Business's anchor is: Business is the ordinary evidence role. It sees Treat State and co-wakers at
its single wake. If it wakes alone, it may inspect exactly one wake result belonging to one chosen
other player; for a multi-wake target, the app reveals one randomly selected result and does not
reveal how many additional wake results the target has. The interaction is healthy only if those two
mechanics create more interpretable evidence rather than a hidden exception that players cannot
reason about.

Cheese Thief lens. Treat the pair as two nodes in a wake-time evidence graph. Trouble contributes 1
d12; Business contributes 1 d12. Test no-overlap, ordinary overlap, and theft-window overlap
configurations. In each configuration, record who can truthfully report PRESENT, MISSING, co-wakers,
and any state change. The pair should preserve the central before/after puzzle even when role
actions add information or change allegiance.

Among Us lens. Ask which previously reliable tell this pair makes ambiguous. Trouble's special
strength is creates legitimate card-state ambiguity and, on theft-window collision, can become a
thematically clean Follower-like accomplice without adding a ninth role; Business's is forms the
baseline evidence network against which special-role claims and hostile stories can be tested. The
design goal is not to make evidence useless. It is to force the table to add one more question
before treating a signal as conclusive. If the pair makes every observation meaningless, it is too
noisy; if one role simply prints the other's guilt, it is too deterministic.

Information asymmetry. Trouble can directly know own wake evidence; exact identities of the two
positions swapped; no knowledge of either swapped card; if converted, the fact of conversion and
historical Raider identity. Business can directly know own wake hour; Treat State; co-wakers; when
alone, one inspected wake result from a chosen player; no direct role scan and no allegiance scan.
Neither player inherits the other's private facts merely because the roles interact. A bot,
reconnect path, spectator, or result preview must enforce the same boundary. Public statements are
claims until independently corroborated or revealed in post-game results.

Claim dynamics. A typical Trouble claim may involve can truthfully reveal the swap while withholding
allegiance change; a loyal Trouble can use swap facts to explain why role claims may no longer match
Current Cards; a converted Trouble can exploit the same uncertainty to shield the Raider. A typical
Business claim may involve simple claims about hour, Treat State, co-wakers, and optional inspected
result. Because Bandits can imitate this structure, credibility must come from consistency and
corroboration rather than uniqueness. During Morning Business, the pair should generate cross-
examination rather than forced confession. Useful questions are whether claimed hours match
witnessed co-wakers, whether Treat State is chronologically possible, whether a card moved between
the relevant timestamps, and whether a claimed trust relation is historical or current.

State-change discipline. If Dooker or Trouble moves either player's Current Card, both Trouble and
Business still resolve scheduled Starting Role actions. If Trouble or Hunter has a player-bound
allegiance conversion, that conversion stays with the player. If one of the pair is historical
Raider, Raider status stays with that player. This rule prevents the pair from creating retroactive
contradictions and makes the Trail of Trouble replay intelligible.

Vote pressure. Trouble's voting doctrine is: loyal Trouble votes for the historical Raider;
converted Trouble protects the historical Raider even if that means accepting personal suspicion.
Trouble’s own capture never substitutes for capturing the Raider.. Business's voting doctrine is:
vote from the reconstructed theft bracket and contradiction network, not from role glamour. A plain
Business claim that fits two other observations can be stronger than a flashy special-role claim
that cannot be reconciled.. Before lock, Guardian may remove one other player from vote-capture
eligibility. The pair must be tested under a clean plurality, a two-way tie, a three-way tie, and a
case where the protected player would otherwise lead. Secondary Bond-Reveal or Hunt Mark effects
occur only after the primary Paw Point catch set is known.

Counterplay for Trouble. The most important risk posed by Business is not simply its power text; it
is how that power can change the interpretation of Trouble's evidence. Trouble should respond by
timestamping facts, keeping the Treat State bracket visible, and refusing to let a single role claim
replace the historical-Raider question. In a hostile Trouble state, the best counterplay is usually
a plausible reinterpretation, not an impossible denial of facts another player personally saw.

Counterplay for Business. The most important risk posed by Trouble is that its story may be partly
true. Business should avoid binary reasoning such as 'one lie means every statement is false' or
'one confirmed fact clears the whole player.' Instead compare independent evidence layers: hour,
Treat State, co-wakers, starting-role capability, Current Card movement, allegiance-changing events,
and voting incentives.

Scenario A: Trouble wakes before Business, and the raid occurs between their first relevant
observations. Trouble reports PRESENT; Business reports MISSING. This brackets the raid without
proving either role claim. Add one public claim from a third player that conflicts with only one
side. The pair should be able to explain why chronology carries more weight than confidence.

Scenario B: Trouble and Business share the theft window. Treat State changes during the window.
Determine whether either role has a conversion trigger, whether the historical Raider is actually
one of them, and which role actions occur after the theft trigger. The rules must make event order
explicit enough that both players can later give internally consistent but strategically different
stories.

Scenario C: a Dooker Trip or Trouble swap changes one participant's Current Card after an earlier
observation. Morning Business now contains a true statement about Starting Role and a different true
statement about current state. The pair passes only if players can reconcile both without treating
one as an automatic lie.

Balance watch. Trouble failure risk: blindness is real; Trouble can accidentally move a Bandit card,
disrupt a useful Business role, or produce a swap that later makes Trouble look responsible for
contradictions it could not predict. Business failure risk: has little privileged power, can be
impersonated easily, and may overvalue a single inspected die when the target is a multi-wake role.
Track whether the pair causes automatic clears, automatic guilt, or excessive narrative branching.
The target is constrained ambiguity: two or three plausible stories at the start of Morning
Business, with enough mechanically grounded questions to reduce that set before Paw Point.

Bot behavior. A Trouble bot follows: maintain a strict distinction between swap-position knowledge
and card-identity ignorance. If converted, the bot may coordinate strategically from known Raider
identity but cannot fabricate having seen the swapped cards.. A Business bot follows: prioritize
timeline consistency, ask direct questions about wake hours and Treat State, and treat inspected
multi-wake results as partial rather than exhaustive. Neither bot may read the other's hidden state
from the authoritative server merely because the simulation has access to it. Their conversation
should be reproducible from authorized facts plus parsed public claims. This is particularly
important in solo play, where omniscient bots would destroy the deduction game.

QA checklist. Seed at least six pair fixtures: no overlap; ordinary overlap before raid; ordinary
overlap after raid; theft-window overlap; one card movement; one Guardian-protected vote. For each
fixture, save starting cards, Current Cards after every move, d12 schedules, relevant d6 results,
Treat State transitions, player-bound statuses, private fact packets, public claims, Paw Point
drafts, locked votes, protection, catch set, secondary effects, and primary winner. The same event
log must replay to the same result.


INTERACTION 20: TROUBLE x SNUGGLER
----------------------------------

Pair thesis. Both roles create player-bound relationships that survive card movement. Their effects
can overlay without transferring when cards swap. Trouble's anchor is: At Trouble’s wake, Trouble
blindly swaps the Current Cards of two distinct other players. If Trouble is awake in the exact
theft window, Trouble becomes In On It before the swap resolves and receives the historical Raider
identity as part of that conversion. Snuggler's anchor is: Before Hour 1, Snuggler chooses two
distinct other players to become Snuggle-Bonded. The bonded players learn each other’s identity as
partners but not each other’s roles. Bond status is player-bound. If one bonded player is caught by
vote or a valid Hunt Mark, the other becomes Bond-Revealed in the final resolution; Bond-Reveal
exposes identity/current card for results but is not itself a Paw Point capture. The interaction is
healthy only if those two mechanics create more interpretable evidence rather than a hidden
exception that players cannot reason about.

Cheese Thief lens. Treat the pair as two nodes in a wake-time evidence graph. Trouble contributes 1
d12; Snuggler contributes 1 d12. Test no-overlap, ordinary overlap, and theft-window overlap
configurations. In each configuration, record who can truthfully report PRESENT, MISSING, co-wakers,
and any state change. The pair should preserve the central before/after puzzle even when role
actions add information or change allegiance.

strength is creates legitimate card-state ambiguity and, on theft-window collision, can become a
thematically clean Follower-like accomplice without adding a ninth role; Snuggler's is creates a
human trust channel that is mechanically real but alignment-neutral, making social alliances less
diagnostic and producing useful ambiguity without adding another hostile faction. The design goal is
not to make evidence useless. It is to force the table to add one more question before treating a
signal as conclusive. If the pair makes every observation meaningless, it is too noisy; if one role
simply prints the other's guilt, it is too deterministic.

historical Raider identity. Snuggler can directly know the selected bonded pair; own normal wake
evidence; no alignment information about the pair unless obtained elsewhere. Neither player inherits
the other's private facts merely because the roles interact. A bot, reconnect path, spectator, or
result preview must enforce the same boundary. Public statements are claims until independently
corroborated or revealed in post-game results.

allegiance change; a loyal Trouble can use swap facts to explain why role claims may no longer match
Current Cards; a converted Trouble can exploit the same uncertainty to shield the Raider. A typical
Snuggler claim may involve can reveal or conceal the pair. Bonded players may explain cooperation by
claiming the bond, but a bond claim never clears either player because one or both may be hostile.

Useful questions are whether claimed hours match witnessed co-wakers, whether Treat State is
chronologically possible, whether a card moved between the relevant timestamps, and whether a

INTERACTION 21: TROUBLE x HUNTER
--------------------------------

Pair thesis. Trouble may become an accomplice while Hunter may independently convert. The hostile
side can therefore grow through two different triggers, making event order and private status
tracking essential. Trouble's anchor is: At Trouble’s wake, Trouble blindly swaps the Current Cards
of two distinct other players. If Trouble is awake in the exact theft window, Trouble becomes In On
It before the swap resolves and receives the historical Raider identity as part of that conversion.
Hunter's anchor is: Hunter and Guardian recognize each other before Hour 1. Hunter wakes twice. On
the first wake that overlaps any starting Bandit’s scheduled wake, Hunter rolls d6 once; 1–2 creates
a permanent player-bound Bandit-side conversion. After Hunter’s later chronological wake, a still-
loyal Hunter places one Hunt Mark. If loyal Hunter is caught in final vote resolution, the mark
catches its target only if that target is Bandit-aligned at resolution. Converted Hunter loses Hunt
Mark effect. The interaction is healthy only if those two mechanics create more interpretable
evidence rather than a hidden exception that players cannot reason about.

Cheese Thief lens. Treat the pair as two nodes in a wake-time evidence graph. Trouble contributes 1
d12; Hunter contributes 2 distinct d12s. Test no-overlap, ordinary overlap, and theft-window overlap
configurations. In each configuration, record who can truthfully report PRESENT, MISSING, co-wakers,
and any state change. The pair should preserve the central before/after puzzle even when role
actions add information or change allegiance.

Among Us lens. Ask which previously reliable tell this pair makes ambiguous. Trouble's special
strength is creates legitimate card-state ambiguity and, on theft-window collision, can become a
thematically clean Follower-like accomplice without adding a ninth role; Hunter's is two wake
windows, a genuine trust anchor, and a conditional anti-Bandit retaliation tool when loyal. The
design goal is not to make evidence useless. It is to force the table to add one more question
before treating a signal as conclusive. If the pair makes every observation meaningless, it is too
noisy; if one role simply prints the other's guilt, it is too deterministic.

Information asymmetry. Trouble can directly know own wake evidence; exact identities of the two
positions swapped; no knowledge of either swapped card; if converted, the fact of conversion and
historical Raider identity. Hunter can directly know Guardian identity at setup; two wake windows
and observations; own conversion result; Hunt Mark target if loyal. Guardian never receives Hunter’s
conversion result.. Neither player inherits the other's private facts merely because the roles
interact. A bot, reconnect path, spectator, or result preview must enforce the same boundary. Public
statements are claims until independently corroborated or revealed in post-game results.

Claim dynamics. A typical Trouble claim may involve can truthfully reveal the swap while withholding
allegiance change; a loyal Trouble can use swap facts to explain why role claims may no longer match
Current Cards; a converted Trouble can exploit the same uncertainty to shield the Raider. A typical
Hunter claim may involve can truthfully claim Guardian recognition even after conversion. This is
deliberately dangerous because a once-valid trust relationship can become stale.. During Morning
Business, the pair should generate cross-examination rather than forced confession. Useful questions
are whether claimed hours match witnessed co-wakers, whether Treat State is chronologically
possible, whether a card moved between the relevant timestamps, and whether a claimed trust relation
is historical or current.

Hunter still resolve scheduled Starting Role actions. If Trouble or Hunter has a player-bound
allegiance conversion, that conversion stays with the player. If one of the pair is historical
Raider, Raider status stays with that player. This rule prevents the pair from creating retroactive
contradictions and makes the Trail of Trouble replay intelligible.

Vote pressure. Trouble's voting doctrine is: loyal Trouble votes for the historical Raider;
converted Trouble protects the historical Raider even if that means accepting personal suspicion.
Trouble’s own capture never substitutes for capturing the Raider.. Hunter's voting doctrine is:
loyal Hunter votes toward Raider and can strategically accept being caught if a high-confidence Hunt
Mark would add a Bandit-aligned target. Converted Hunter protects Raider and should exploit
Guardian’s stale trust without claiming information the role never received.. Before lock, Guardian
may remove one other player from vote-capture eligibility. The pair must be tested under a clean
plurality, a two-way tie, a three-way tie, and a case where the protected player would otherwise
lead. Secondary Bond-Reveal or Hunt Mark effects occur only after the primary Paw Point catch set is
known.

how that power can change the interpretation of Trouble's evidence. Trouble should respond by
timestamping facts, keeping the Treat State bracket visible, and refusing to let a single role claim
replace the historical-Raider question. In a hostile Trouble state, the best counterplay is usually
a plausible reinterpretation, not an impossible denial of facts another player personally saw.

confirmed fact clears the whole player.' Instead compare independent evidence layers: hour, Treat
State, co-wakers, starting-role capability, Current Card movement, allegiance-changing events, and

Scenario C: a Dooker Trip or Trouble swap changes one participant's Current Card after an earlier

INTERACTION 22: TROUBLE x GUARDIAN
----------------------------------

conversion status. The interaction is healthy only if those two mechanics create more interpretable
evidence rather than a hidden exception that players cannot reason about.
