INTERACTION 23: BUSINESS x SNUGGLER
-----------------------------------

Pair thesis. Business should treat a bond claim as relationship context, then return to ordinary
evidence. A bond neither validates nor invalidates a wake story. Business's anchor is: Business is
the ordinary evidence role. It sees Treat State and co-wakers at its single wake. If it wakes alone,
it may inspect exactly one wake result belonging to one chosen other player; for a multi-wake
target, the app reveals one randomly selected result and does not reveal how many additional wake
results the target has. Snuggler's anchor is: Before Hour 1, Snuggler chooses two distinct other
players to become Snuggle-Bonded. The bonded players learn each other’s identity as partners but not
each other’s roles. Bond status is player-bound. If one bonded player is caught by vote or a valid
Hunt Mark, the other becomes Bond-Revealed in the final resolution; Bond-Reveal exposes
identity/current card for results but is not itself a Paw Point capture. The interaction is healthy
only if those two mechanics create more interpretable evidence rather than a hidden exception that
players cannot reason about.

Cheese Thief lens. Treat the pair as two nodes in a wake-time evidence graph. Business contributes 1
d12; Snuggler contributes 1 d12. Test no-overlap, ordinary overlap, and theft-window overlap
configurations. In each configuration, record who can truthfully report PRESENT, MISSING, co-wakers,
and any state change. The pair should preserve the central before/after puzzle even when role
actions add information or change allegiance.

Among Us lens. Ask which previously reliable tell this pair makes ambiguous. Business's special
strength is forms the baseline evidence network against which special-role claims and hostile
stories can be tested; Snuggler's is creates a human trust channel that is mechanically real but
alignment-neutral, making social alliances less diagnostic and producing useful ambiguity without
adding another hostile faction. The design goal is not to make evidence useless. It is to force the
table to add one more question before treating a signal as conclusive. If the pair makes every
observation meaningless, it is too noisy; if one role simply prints the other's guilt, it is too
deterministic.

Information asymmetry. Business can directly know own wake hour; Treat State; co-wakers; when alone,
one inspected wake result from a chosen player; no direct role scan and no allegiance scan. Snuggler
can directly know the selected bonded pair; own normal wake evidence; no alignment information about
the pair unless obtained elsewhere. Neither player inherits the other's private facts merely because
the roles interact. A bot, reconnect path, spectator, or result preview must enforce the same
boundary. Public statements are claims until independently corroborated or revealed in post-game
results.

Claim dynamics. A typical Business claim may involve simple claims about hour, Treat State, co-
wakers, and optional inspected result. Because Bandits can imitate this structure, credibility must
come from consistency and corroboration rather than uniqueness. A typical Snuggler claim may involve
can reveal or conceal the pair. Bonded players may explain cooperation by claiming the bond, but a
bond claim never clears either player because one or both may be hostile. During Morning Business,
the pair should generate cross-examination rather than forced confession. Useful questions are
whether claimed hours match witnessed co-wakers, whether Treat State is chronologically possible,
whether a card moved between the relevant timestamps, and whether a claimed trust relation is
historical or current.

State-change discipline. If Dooker or Trouble moves either player's Current Card, both Business and
Snuggler still resolve scheduled Starting Role actions. If Trouble or Hunter has a player-bound
allegiance conversion, that conversion stays with the player. If one of the pair is historical
Raider, Raider status stays with that player. This rule prevents the pair from creating retroactive
contradictions and makes the Trail of Trouble replay intelligible.

Vote pressure. Business's voting doctrine is: vote from the reconstructed theft bracket and
contradiction network, not from role glamour. A plain Business claim that fits two other
observations can be stronger than a flashy special-role claim that cannot be reconciled.. Snuggler's
voting doctrine is: vote normally toward the historical Raider. Consider the secondary Bond-Reveal
consequence because catching one bonded player can expose the other, but do not treat exposure as
automatic primary victory unless another rule also catches the Raider.. Before lock, Guardian may
remove one other player from vote-capture eligibility. The pair must be tested under a clean
plurality, a two-way tie, a three-way tie, and a case where the protected player would otherwise
lead. Secondary Bond-Reveal or Hunt Mark effects occur only after the primary Paw Point catch set is
known.

Counterplay for Business. The most important risk posed by Snuggler is not simply its power text; it
is how that power can change the interpretation of Business's evidence. Business should respond by
timestamping facts, keeping the Treat State bracket visible, and refusing to let a single role claim
replace the historical-Raider question. In a hostile Business state, the best counterplay is usually
a plausible reinterpretation, not an impossible denial of facts another player personally saw.

Counterplay for Snuggler. The most important risk posed by Business is that its story may be partly
true. Snuggler should avoid binary reasoning such as 'one lie means every statement is false' or
'one confirmed fact clears the whole player.' Instead compare independent evidence layers: hour,
Treat State, co-wakers, starting-role capability, Current Card movement, allegiance-changing events,
and voting incentives.

Scenario A: Business wakes before Snuggler, and the raid occurs between their first relevant
observations. Business reports PRESENT; Snuggler reports MISSING. This brackets the raid without
proving either role claim. Add one public claim from a third player that conflicts with only one
side. The pair should be able to explain why chronology carries more weight than confidence.

Scenario B: Business and Snuggler share the theft window. Treat State changes during the window.
Determine whether either role has a conversion trigger, whether the historical Raider is actually
one of them, and which role actions occur after the theft trigger. The rules must make event order
explicit enough that both players can later give internally consistent but strategically different
stories.

Scenario C: a Dooker Trip or Trouble swap changes one participant's Current Card after an earlier
observation. Morning Business now contains a true statement about Starting Role and a different true
statement about current state. The pair passes only if players can reconcile both without treating
one as an automatic lie.

Balance watch. Business failure risk: has little privileged power, can be impersonated easily, and
may overvalue a single inspected die when the target is a multi-wake role. Snuggler failure risk: a
bond can connect Raider to a Business player, connect two Business players who then overtrust each
other, or become strategically irrelevant if neither bonded player is pressured. Track whether the
pair causes automatic clears, automatic guilt, or excessive narrative branching. The target is
constrained ambiguity: two or three plausible stories at the start of Morning Business, with enough
mechanically grounded questions to reduce that set before Paw Point.

Bot behavior. A Business bot follows: prioritize timeline consistency, ask direct questions about
wake hours and Treat State, and treat inspected multi-wake results as partial rather than
exhaustive. A Snuggler bot follows: use the bond as relationship evidence only; never assign
negative or positive alignment weight merely because players are bonded. Neither bot may read the
other's hidden state from the authoritative server merely because the simulation has access to it.
Their conversation should be reproducible from authorized facts plus parsed public claims. This is
particularly important in solo play, where omniscient bots would destroy the deduction game.

QA checklist. Seed at least six pair fixtures: no overlap; ordinary overlap before raid; ordinary
overlap after raid; theft-window overlap; one card movement; one Guardian-protected vote. For each
fixture, save starting cards, Current Cards after every move, d12 schedules, relevant d6 results,
Treat State transitions, player-bound statuses, private fact packets, public claims, Paw Point
drafts, locked votes, protection, catch set, secondary effects, and primary winner. The same event
log must replay to the same result.


INTERACTION 24: BUSINESS x HUNTER
---------------------------------

Pair thesis. Business can corroborate Hunter’s two claimed hours only partially. Hunter’s two wakes
are mechanically plausible, but loyalty remains a separate question after possible overlap
conversion. Business's anchor is: Business is the ordinary evidence role. It sees Treat State and
co-wakers at its single wake. If it wakes alone, it may inspect exactly one wake result belonging to
one chosen other player; for a multi-wake target, the app reveals one randomly selected result and
does not reveal how many additional wake results the target has. Hunter's anchor is: Hunter and
Guardian recognize each other before Hour 1. Hunter wakes twice. On the first wake that overlaps any
starting Bandit’s scheduled wake, Hunter rolls d6 once; 1–2 creates a permanent player-bound Bandit-
side conversion. After Hunter’s later chronological wake, a still-loyal Hunter places one Hunt Mark.
If loyal Hunter is caught in final vote resolution, the mark catches its target only if that target
is Bandit-aligned at resolution. Converted Hunter loses Hunt Mark effect. The interaction is healthy
only if those two mechanics create more interpretable evidence rather than a hidden exception that
players cannot reason about.

Cheese Thief lens. Treat the pair as two nodes in a wake-time evidence graph. Business contributes 1
d12; Hunter contributes 2 distinct d12s. Test no-overlap, ordinary overlap, and theft-window overlap
configurations. In each configuration, record who can truthfully report PRESENT, MISSING, co-wakers,
and any state change. The pair should preserve the central before/after puzzle even when role
actions add information or change allegiance.

strength is forms the baseline evidence network against which special-role claims and hostile

can truthfully claim Guardian recognition even after conversion. This is deliberately dangerous

INTERACTION 25: BUSINESS x GUARDIAN
-----------------------------------

Pair thesis. Business evidence should drive Guardian’s protection choice. Guardian provides a vote
tool, while Business provides the timeline that tells the table where that tool is safest.
Business's anchor is: Business is the ordinary evidence role. It sees Treat State and co-wakers at
its single wake. If it wakes alone, it may inspect exactly one wake result belonging to one chosen
other player; for a multi-wake target, the app reveals one randomly selected result and does not
reveal how many additional wake results the target has. Guardian's anchor is: Guardian and Hunter
recognize each other before Hour 1. Guardian wakes once normally. Immediately before Paw Point
locks, Guardian privately protects one other player. The protected player is removed from vote-
capture eligibility for that resolution; votes on the protected player do not determine the top
eligible target, so the highest remaining eligible total is used. Guardian never learns Hunter’s
conversion status. The interaction is healthy only if those two mechanics create more interpretable
evidence rather than a hidden exception that players cannot reason about.

Cheese Thief lens. Treat the pair as two nodes in a wake-time evidence graph. Business contributes 1
d12; Guardian contributes 1 d12. Test no-overlap, ordinary overlap, and theft-window overlap
configurations. In each configuration, record who can truthfully report PRESENT, MISSING, co-wakers,
and any state change. The pair should preserve the central before/after puzzle even when role
actions add information or change allegiance.

Among Us lens. Ask which previously reliable tell this pair makes ambiguous. Business's special
strength is forms the baseline evidence network against which special-role claims and hostile
stories can be tested; Guardian's is can reshape the decisive vote and provides one genuine setup
relationship that helps anchor discussion. The design goal is not to make evidence useless. It is to
force the table to add one more question before treating a signal as conclusive. If the pair makes
every observation meaningless, it is too noisy; if one role simply prints the other's guilt, it is
too deterministic.

Information asymmetry. Business can directly know own wake hour; Treat State; co-wakers; when alone,
one inspected wake result from a chosen player; no direct role scan and no allegiance scan. Guardian
can directly know starting Hunter identity; own wake evidence; own chosen protected target; no
direct information about Hunter conversion. Neither player inherits the other's private facts merely
because the roles interact. A bot, reconnect path, spectator, or result preview must enforce the
same boundary. Public statements are claims until independently corroborated or revealed in post-
game results.

Claim dynamics. A typical Business claim may involve simple claims about hour, Treat State, co-
wakers, and optional inspected result. Because Bandits can imitate this structure, credibility must
come from consistency and corroboration rather than uniqueness. A typical Guardian claim may involve
can reveal the original Hunter relationship without revealing intended protection. Guardian should
phrase trust carefully because recognition proves Starting Role, not dawn allegiance.. During

chronologically possible, whether a card moved between the relevant timestamps, and whether a

Raider, Raider status stays with that player. This rule prevents the pair from creating retroactive
contradictions and makes the Trail of Trouble replay intelligible.

contradiction network, not from role glamour. A plain Business claim that fits two other
observations can be stronger than a flashy special-role claim that cannot be reconciled.. Guardian's
voting doctrine is: Guardian still casts a normal Paw Point. Protection and vote are separate
choices. Protect the player whose survival most improves the probability of catching Raider, not
automatically Hunter.. Before lock, Guardian may remove one other player from vote-capture
eligibility. The pair must be tested under a clean plurality, a two-way tie, a three-way tie, and a
case where the protected player would otherwise lead. Secondary Bond-Reveal or Hunt Mark effects
occur only after the primary Paw Point catch set is known.

Counterplay for Business. The most important risk posed by Guardian is not simply its power text; it
is how that power can change the interpretation of Business's evidence. Business should respond by
timestamping facts, keeping the Treat State bracket visible, and refusing to let a single role claim
replace the historical-Raider question. In a hostile Business state, the best counterplay is usually
a plausible reinterpretation, not an impossible denial of facts another player personally saw.

Counterplay for Guardian. The most important risk posed by Business is that its story may be partly
true. Guardian should avoid binary reasoning such as 'one lie means every statement is false' or
'one confirmed fact clears the whole player.' Instead compare independent evidence layers: hour,
Treat State, co-wakers, starting-role capability, Current Card movement, allegiance-changing events,

Scenario B: Business and Guardian share the theft window. Treat State changes during the window.

one of them, and which role actions occur after the theft trigger. The rules must make event order

Scenario C: a Dooker Trip or Trouble swap changes one participant's Current Card after an earlier
observation. Morning Business now contains a true statement about Starting Role and a different true

one as an automatic lie.

Balance watch. Business failure risk: has little privileged power, can be impersonated easily, and
may overvalue a single inspected die when the target is a multi-wake role. Guardian failure risk:
protection can save the Raider or converted Hunter, can redirect capture onto an innocent runner-up,

Bot behavior. A Business bot follows: prioritize timeline consistency, ask direct questions about

bot may read the other's hidden state from the authoritative server merely because the simulation
has access to it. Their conversation should be reproducible from authorized facts plus parsed public
claims. This is particularly important in solo play, where omniscient bots would destroy the
deduction game.

log must replay to the same result.

INTERACTION 27: SNUGGLER x GUARDIAN
-----------------------------------

players cannot reason about.

Cheese Thief lens. Treat the pair as two nodes in a wake-time evidence graph. Snuggler contributes 1
d12; Guardian contributes 1 d12. Test no-overlap, ordinary overlap, and theft-window overlap
configurations. In each configuration, record who can truthfully report PRESENT, MISSING, co-wakers,
and any state change. The pair should preserve the central before/after puzzle even when role
actions add information or change allegiance.

Among Us lens. Ask which previously reliable tell this pair makes ambiguous. Snuggler's special
strength is creates a human trust channel that is mechanically real but alignment-neutral, making
social alliances less diagnostic and producing useful ambiguity without adding another hostile
faction; Guardian's is can reshape the decisive vote and provides one genuine setup relationship
that helps anchor discussion. The design goal is not to make evidence useless. It is to force the
table to add one more question before treating a signal as conclusive. If the pair makes every
observation meaningless, it is too noisy; if one role simply prints the other's guilt, it is too
deterministic.

Information asymmetry. Snuggler can directly know the selected bonded pair; own normal wake
evidence; no alignment information about the pair unless obtained elsewhere. Guardian can directly
know starting Hunter identity; own wake evidence; own chosen protected target; no direct information
about Hunter conversion. Neither player inherits the other's private facts merely because the roles
interact. A bot, reconnect path, spectator, or result preview must enforce the same boundary. Public
statements are claims until independently corroborated or revealed in post-game results.

Claim dynamics. A typical Snuggler claim may involve can reveal or conceal the pair. Bonded players
may explain cooperation by claiming the bond, but a bond claim never clears either player because
one or both may be hostile. A typical Guardian claim may involve can reveal the original Hunter
relationship without revealing intended protection. Guardian should phrase trust carefully because
recognition proves Starting Role, not dawn allegiance.. During Morning Business, the pair should
generate cross-examination rather than forced confession. Useful questions are whether claimed hours
match witnessed co-wakers, whether Treat State is chronologically possible, whether a card moved
between the relevant timestamps, and whether a claimed trust relation is historical or current.

State-change discipline. If Dooker or Trouble moves either player's Current Card, both Snuggler and
Guardian still resolve scheduled Starting Role actions. If Trouble or Hunter has a player-bound
allegiance conversion, that conversion stays with the player. If one of the pair is historical
Raider, Raider status stays with that player. This rule prevents the pair from creating retroactive
contradictions and makes the Trail of Trouble replay intelligible.

Vote pressure. Snuggler's voting doctrine is: vote normally toward the historical Raider. Consider
the secondary Bond-Reveal consequence because catching one bonded player can expose the other, but
do not treat exposure as automatic primary victory unless another rule also catches the Raider..
Guardian's voting doctrine is: Guardian still casts a normal Paw Point. Protection and vote are
separate choices. Protect the player whose survival most improves the probability of catching
Raider, not automatically Hunter.. Before lock, Guardian may remove one other player from vote-
capture eligibility. The pair must be tested under a clean plurality, a two-way tie, a three-way
tie, and a case where the protected player would otherwise lead. Secondary Bond-Reveal or Hunt Mark
effects occur only after the primary Paw Point catch set is known.

Counterplay for Snuggler. The most important risk posed by Guardian is not simply its power text; it
is how that power can change the interpretation of Snuggler's evidence. Snuggler should respond by
timestamping facts, keeping the Treat State bracket visible, and refusing to let a single role claim
replace the historical-Raider question. In a hostile Snuggler state, the best counterplay is usually
a plausible reinterpretation, not an impossible denial of facts another player personally saw.

Counterplay for Guardian. The most important risk posed by Snuggler is that its story may be partly
true. Guardian should avoid binary reasoning such as 'one lie means every statement is false' or
'one confirmed fact clears the whole player.' Instead compare independent evidence layers: hour,
Treat State, co-wakers, starting-role capability, Current Card movement, allegiance-changing events,
and voting incentives.

Scenario A: Snuggler wakes before Guardian, and the raid occurs between their first relevant
observations. Snuggler reports PRESENT; Guardian reports MISSING. This brackets the raid without
proving either role claim. Add one public claim from a third player that conflicts with only one
side. The pair should be able to explain why chronology carries more weight than confidence.

Scenario B: Snuggler and Guardian share the theft window. Treat State changes during the window.
Determine whether either role has a conversion trigger, whether the historical Raider is actually
one of them, and which role actions occur after the theft trigger. The rules must make event order
explicit enough that both players can later give internally consistent but strategically different
stories.

Scenario C: a Dooker Trip or Trouble swap changes one participant's Current Card after an earlier
observation. Morning Business now contains a true statement about Starting Role and a different true
statement about current state. The pair passes only if players can reconcile both without treating
one as an automatic lie.

Balance watch. Snuggler failure risk: a bond can connect Raider to a Business player, connect two
Business players who then overtrust each other, or become strategically irrelevant if neither bonded
player is pressured. Guardian failure risk: protection can save the Raider or converted Hunter, can
redirect capture onto an innocent runner-up, and can turn stale trust into a direct hostile
advantage. Track whether the pair causes automatic clears, automatic guilt, or excessive narrative
branching. The target is constrained ambiguity: two or three plausible stories at the start of
Morning Business, with enough mechanically grounded questions to reduce that set before Paw Point.

Bot behavior. A Snuggler bot follows: use the bond as relationship evidence only; never assign
negative or positive alignment weight merely because players are bonded. A Guardian bot follows:
evaluate protection after discussion, avoid self-protection, and model the redirected top-vote
outcome rather than merely shielding the most trusted player. Neither bot may read the other's
hidden state from the authoritative server merely because the simulation has access to it. Their
conversation should be reproducible from authorized facts plus parsed public claims. This is
particularly important in solo play, where omniscient bots would destroy the deduction game.

QA checklist. Seed at least six pair fixtures: no overlap; ordinary overlap before raid; ordinary
overlap after raid; theft-window overlap; one card movement; one Guardian-protected vote. For each
fixture, save starting cards, Current Cards after every move, d12 schedules, relevant d6 results,
Treat State transitions, player-bound statuses, private fact packets, public claims, Paw Point
drafts, locked votes, protection, catch set, secondary effects, and primary winner. The same event
log must replay to the same result.


INTERACTION 28: HUNTER x GUARDIAN
---------------------------------

Pair thesis. This is intentionally a stale-trust pair. Recognition is certain at setup; Hunter
loyalty is not certain at dawn. The pair embodies the Among Us lesson that a once-conclusive read
can be invalidated by enabled role rules. Hunter's anchor is: Hunter and Guardian recognize each
other before Hour 1. Hunter wakes twice. On the first wake that overlaps any starting Bandit’s
scheduled wake, Hunter rolls d6 once; 1–2 creates a permanent player-bound Bandit-side conversion.
After Hunter’s later chronological wake, a still-loyal Hunter places one Hunt Mark. If loyal Hunter
is caught in final vote resolution, the mark catches its target only if that target is Bandit-
aligned at resolution. Converted Hunter loses Hunt Mark effect. Guardian's anchor is: Guardian and
Hunter recognize each other before Hour 1. Guardian wakes once normally. Immediately before Paw
Point locks, Guardian privately protects one other player. The protected player is removed from
vote-capture eligibility for that resolution; votes on the protected player do not determine the top
eligible target, so the highest remaining eligible total is used. Guardian never learns Hunter’s
conversion status. The interaction is healthy only if those two mechanics create more interpretable
evidence rather than a hidden exception that players cannot reason about.

Cheese Thief lens. Treat the pair as two nodes in a wake-time evidence graph. Hunter contributes 2
distinct d12s; Guardian contributes 1 d12. Test no-overlap, ordinary overlap, and theft-window
overlap configurations. In each configuration, record who can truthfully report PRESENT, MISSING,
co-wakers, and any state change. The pair should preserve the central before/after puzzle even when
role actions add information or change allegiance.

deterministic.

observations; own conversion result; Hunt Mark target if loyal. Guardian never receives Hunter’s

PART VI — PLAYER-COUNT AND BALANCE GUIDANCE
===========================================

4 players: 1 Bandit, 3 Business, Dooker, Hunter, Guardian in the seven-card Active Set (four dealt +
three Hammock). Keep conversion-heavy combinations uncommon at this count. These are Active Set
recipes, not guaranteed dealt-role lists; three cards remain in the Hammock after the deal.

5 players: 1 Bandit, 3 Business, Dooker, Itchy, Trouble, Snuggler in the eight-card Active Set.
These are Active Set recipes, not guaranteed dealt-role lists; three cards remain in the Hammock
after the deal.

6 players: 1 Bandit, 3 Business, Dooker, Itchy, Trouble, Hunter, Guardian in the nine-card Active
Set. These are Active Set recipes, not guaranteed dealt-role lists; three cards remain in the
Hammock after the deal.

7 players: 2 Bandit, 3 Business, Dooker, Trouble, Snuggler, Hunter, Guardian in the ten-card Active
Set. These are Active Set recipes, not guaranteed dealt-role lists; three cards remain in the
Hammock after the deal.

8 players: 2 Bandit, 4 Business, Dooker, Itchy, Trouble, Hunter, Guardian in the eleven-card Active
Set. These are Active Set recipes, not guaranteed dealt-role lists; three cards remain in the
Hammock after the deal.

9 players: 2 Bandit, 4 Business, Dooker, Itchy, Trouble, Snuggler, Hunter, Guardian in the twelve-
card Active Set. These are Active Set recipes, not guaranteed dealt-role lists; three cards remain
in the Hammock after the deal.

10 players: 2 Bandit, 5 Business, Dooker, Itchy, Trouble, Snuggler, Hunter, Guardian in the
thirteen-card Active Set. These are Active Set recipes, not guaranteed dealt-role lists; three cards
remain in the Hammock after the deal.

Balance should be evaluated by player count because the same mechanic has different information
value in a sparse or crowded clock. With fewer players, a lone wake is common and a single hard fact
can dominate. With more players, collisions and coordinated hostile claims become more common, but
additional corroboration is also available. The role recipes intentionally keep a substantial
Business baseline so the game always contains an ordinary claim vocabulary that Bandits can imitate
and special roles can cross-check.

Do not balance by forcing every role into every match. Cheese Thief's research shows that player-
count topology can change the social structure of the conspiracy, and Among Us shows that role
density changes what evidence means. Ferret Frenzy should use Active Set recipes and Hammock
uncertainty to vary the role mix while keeping the eight-role library fixed.


PART VII — IMPLEMENTATION AND BOT INVARIANTS
============================================

- Starting Role is immutable and alone determines scheduled role actions.
- Current Card movement never moves historical Raider, Trouble conversion, Hunter conversion, Snuggle Bond, Guardian protection, or Hunt Mark statuses.
- d12 raw values are private; only entitled derived wake observations are sent to other clients.
- d6 raw values are private unless a rule explicitly reveals the result after the round.
- No client receives another player’s hidden action prompt merely to animate the night.
- Public chat claims are never validated against hidden server truth.
- Quick Role Claim is a statement builder, not an authentication system.
- Bots choose from authorized facts and public claims; their authoritative server access cannot leak hidden truth into behavior.
- Guardian protection modifies Paw Point capture eligibility only.
- Tied highest eligible Paw Point totals catch all tied players.
- Historical Raider capture determines primary winner after valid capture effects resolve.
- Post-game replay may explain hidden events only after the round and according to host privacy settings; it cannot retroactively alter what players were allowed to know.

The frontend package inspected for this revision already separates Starting Role, Current Card,
player-bound statuses, shared Morning Business, simultaneous voting, and specialized bot role
brains. Revision 3.1 therefore treats those as existing architecture and focuses on exact rules
semantics. If later code differs, the intended implementation task is to make code match this
dossier rather than silently changing the dossier back to accommodate an accidental edge behavior.


PART VIII — AUTHORITATIVE REVISION 3.1 QUICK RULES
==================================================

- Eight roles only: Bandit, Dooker, Itchy, Trouble, Business, Snuggler, Hunter, Guardian.
- Two dice only: d12 = Burrow Hour; d6 = Itchy activation, Hunter conversion, Bandit Raider tie break.
- Bandit: starting Bandits know roster; earliest starting-Bandit wake is historical Raider and steals.
- Dooker: three distinct wakes; first observation, second one current-allegiance Glimpse, third one Hammock peek and optional Trip.
- Itchy: d6 5–6 activates second wake and two-player clue containing historical Raider plus a legal decoy.
- Trouble: one wake, blind swap two other Current Cards; if present for theft, converts In On It before swap and learns Raider identity.
- Business: one wake; if genuinely alone, inspect exactly one wake result of one chosen player, not the target’s whole schedule.
- Snuggler: pre-night bonds two other players; bond is relationship only; caught partner can cause Bond-Reveal of the other, not automatic primary capture.
- Hunter: recognizes Guardian; two wakes; first starting-Bandit overlap triggers one d6, 1–2 converts; loyal Hunter marks after later wake and mark can catch Bandit-aligned target only if Hunter is caught.
- Guardian: recognizes Hunter at setup, never learns conversion; protects one other player before Paw Point, removing that player from vote-capture eligibility.
- Same-hour order: wake -> Treat State before action -> Raider theft -> conversion/witness triggers -> Starting-Role actions -> commit.
- Starting Role actions never move with cards. Current Card, Current Allegiance, and historical/player-bound statuses are separate.
- Morning Business is open discussion of claims; the app does not publish guilt scores.
- Paw Point locks simultaneously; after protection, every eligible tied top target is caught.
- Primary Business win requires historical Raider in the final catch set. Catching only accomplices/converts is insufficient.

- Moved Current Cards are not automatically re-revealed to their holders before Results; ordinary current-card-derived allegiance is likewise not player-known unless a mechanic grants that knowledge.
- Earliest-Bandit d6 ties use repeated lowest-only rerolls until one unique lowest result selects Raider.
- Role actions and Paw Point exclude self; the backend validates all target legality even when the UI already filters it.
- Activated Itchy clue delivery may be deferred to Morning Business when both Itchy wakes occur before the theft; this is not an extra wake.
- If Guardian protection leaves no unprotected target with a positive vote total, Paw Point catches nobody.
- Secondary resolution order is Paw Point catch set -> loyal Hunter Hunt Mark -> Snuggle Bond result reveal -> historical Raider primary-win check.
- Bots may not use server-only Current Card/allegiance fields that the equivalent human seat has not been authorized to know.


PART IX — REVISION 3.2 PACKAGE PRESERVATION AND TARGET-LEGALITY PASS
===================================================================

1. Why this pass exists
-----------------------

Revision 3.2 addresses a release-engineering failure mode that is separate from game balance but just as important to a playable build: a dossier pass must not accidentally become a package-reduction pass. Ferret Frenzy is not only its rule text. The playable artifact also depends on its lobby, game shell, scripts, stylesheets, JSON configuration, twenty-four card images, scene artwork, logo and icons, audio cues, bot response libraries, bot reasoning code, tests, documentation, PWA files, and backend-reference configuration. A gameplay review that returns only a revised text file is not an acceptable replacement for a previously supplied complete game package when the requested task is a pass on the game itself.

The preservation rule is therefore explicit: begin from the most complete known-good package, apply dossier-driven changes in place, add new documentation and tests, and remove nothing unless the removal is specifically required by the user or the file is a provably obsolete duplicate whose replacement is present and verified. When there is uncertainty, preserve the file. Disk size is not itself a design goal, but unexpected shrinkage is a warning signal because it can indicate missing assets, bot libraries, audio, card art, or code. Release validation must compare both logical contents and aggregate package size against the chosen baseline.

For this pass, the full Revision 3 second-dossier-pass package is the preservation baseline. Its game directory contains the complete frontend, assets, bot libraries, tests, and documentation. Revision 3.2 carries those forward and adds the new dossier, audit, preservation manifest, integrity test, and small frontend validation changes. The backend endpoint remains referenced exactly as before; no Apps Script source is introduced or rewritten.


2. Preservation hierarchy for future dossier passes
----------------------------------------------------

A future pass should classify every file into one of four categories before packaging. PRESERVE means the file is unchanged and required or potentially required by the working game. MODIFY IN PLACE means the file remains at the same path but receives a tested improvement. ADD means a new file is introduced without displacing existing working content. REMOVE is exceptional and must have an explicit reason recorded in the audit. A general wish to make the package cleaner, smaller, or more elegant is not sufficient reason to remove a game asset or subsystem.

The preservation hierarchy is intentionally conservative. User-created art and audio have the highest protection because they are difficult to reconstruct exactly. Role-card image sets must remain complete. Bot JSON response libraries must retain their full response counts. The lobby and game entry points must remain present. PWA files must remain present. Existing documentation should remain available even when a newer revision becomes authoritative, because older audit notes explain why current behavior exists. Tests should accumulate rather than disappear unless they are made redundant by stronger tests that demonstrably cover the same contract.

When a newer file replaces an older behavioral implementation at the same path, the release audit should describe the change rather than silently omitting the old functionality. When a newer dossier supersedes wording, keep the earlier dossier as a historical reference inside docs if package size is not a concern. This is especially useful in Ferret Frenzy because role interactions have been iterated repeatedly and regressions often come from losing a small rule clarification that looked unimportant in isolation.


3. Package-size validation is a smoke test, not an optimization target
---------------------------------------------------------------------

A completed pass should record baseline ZIP size, baseline uncompressed size, baseline file count, candidate ZIP size, candidate uncompressed size, and candidate file count. These numbers do not prove correctness, but a large unexplained drop must block release until accounted for. Compression can legitimately change when text or code changes, so exact byte equality is not required. The relevant question is whether every expected subsystem is still present.

For Ferret Frenzy, the strongest content checks are structural: all twenty-four card images exist; all four packaged gameplay/lobby audio files remain; the pre-theft and crime-scene images remain; the logo and PWA icons remain; all eight bot JSON libraries remain and still contain 250 responses each; the core lobby and frenzy entry HTML files remain; required JavaScript and CSS remain; the service worker and manifest remain; and the docs directory still contains the gameplay authority and prior pass notes. A release that fails one of these checks is incomplete even if the remaining files run.

Revision 3.2 adds an automated package-integrity test for the most failure-prone preserved resources. The test is not a license to delete anything it does not name. It is a minimum alarm system. The preservation manifest provides the broader comparison record.


4. Paw Point self-target legality
---------------------------------

The dossier has always described Paw Point as one vote for one other eligible player. Revision 3.1 made the self-target prohibition explicit, but the generic frontend voting helper previously accepted an actor and target whenever both IDs were included in the eligible list. The visible vote panel already omitted the player's own seat, so ordinary human use was safe, but helper-level permissiveness created an unnecessary discrepancy between the rule and reusable code.

Revision 3.2 closes that discrepancy. Client voting helpers reject actor-equals-target records by default. The vote-selection function also refuses to store a self target, and the final client submission guard revalidates that the selected target exists among active participants and is not the local player. This does not replace backend validation. It creates defense in depth: visible UI, reusable helper, submission layer, and authoritative server are all expected to agree that self-voting is illegal.

A malformed client, stale browser state, or future refactor must not be able to turn frontend permissiveness into a new rule. Server-side `vote.cast` remains authoritative and should reject a self target even if a modified client submits one.


5. One vote per actor and duplicate-record handling
---------------------------------------------------

A simultaneous Paw Point is not a stream of unlimited ballots. Each active player contributes one locked vote. Reusable tally logic should therefore treat repeated records from the same actor as invalid or idempotent rather than counting all of them. Revision 3.2 helper logic keeps at most one valid vote per actor within a tally operation. The authoritative backend should continue to enforce the stronger stateful rule that a locked player cannot replace or add another vote.

The helper behavior is primarily a safety net for tests, replay analysis, and any future offline-compatible resolver. It prevents a duplicated event record from inflating a target total. If replay contains a duplicate transport event but only one authoritative vote lock, the authoritative locked vote wins; replay code should deduplicate by event/action identity before presentation.


6. Guardian protection and the zero-capture Paw Point case
----------------------------------------------------------

Guardian protection removes one other player from Paw Point capture eligibility for that resolution. Votes cast toward the protected player remain historical vote records but are excluded from eligible capture totals. An important consequence is that protection can produce no Paw Point capture at all. If every valid vote is directed at protected targets, there is no unprotected player with a positive tally. The resolver must return an empty Paw Point catch set. It must not promote an unvoted player merely because that player is technically eligible.

Revision 3.2 codifies this in helper behavior and automated tests. Top-target computation removes protected target totals, ignores zero/nonpositive totals, and returns an empty list when nothing remains. If two or more unprotected players share the highest positive total, all remain caught as before. Protection therefore changes eligibility, not the historical vote record and not the meaning of ties.

This edge matters for Hunter and Snuggler resolution. An empty Paw Point catch set means loyal Hunter's conditional catch does not trigger from Hunter being Paw Point-caught unless Hunter was caught by another valid mechanism specified by the rules. Bond-Reveal likewise requires a qualifying caught bonded player. The absence of a Paw Point catch should propagate naturally through secondary resolution rather than being patched by inventing a substitute target.


7. Role-action target legality at the presentation layer
--------------------------------------------------------

Every current role action that targets players uses another player, and the two-target actions require two distinct other players. Dooker Glimpse targets one other player. Dooker Trip places the inspected Hammock card onto one player other than Dooker. Trouble swaps two distinct other players. Business solo inspection selects another player. Snuggler bonds two distinct other players. Loyal Hunter marks another player. Guardian protects another player. None of these powers targets self under the current dossier.

The frontend previously relied partly on an `excludeSelf` flag supplied with an action prompt. That is useful metadata, but it should not be the only presentation safeguard for established base-role rules. Revision 3.2 maintains a client-side list of the existing other-player-only action types so the local seat is filtered even if a prompt accidentally omits the flag. The server remains authoritative and must still reject self-targets and repeated targets where the rule requires distinct players.

The two-target selection widget already prevents selecting the same displayed player twice because a selected button toggles rather than creating duplicate entries. Revision 3.2 treats that behavior as convenience only. The action resolver must independently assert `targetA != targetB`, both targets exist, neither is the actor, and both are currently legal active participants.


8. Protected targets remain valid vote destinations
---------------------------------------------------

Guardian protection is intentionally hidden procedural state until resolution unless the backend rules reveal it. The voting UI should not necessarily remove a protected player from the selectable list, because the dossier states that votes cast toward a protected player remain recorded for replay and are then excluded from capture eligibility. This means target validity for CASTING a vote and eligibility for being CAUGHT are not identical concepts.

Revision 3.2 therefore does not change the basic visible candidate list based on protection. The separate resolution layer removes protected targets from the catch calculation. This preserves the strategic redirection effect described in the Guardian chapter: a group can concentrate on a protected suspect and end with no capture or a different eligible leader, depending on the distribution of the remaining votes.

Future code should avoid a tempting shortcut where `protected` is treated as `cannotReceiveVotes`. That shortcut would change public information and player behavior before lock. Protection belongs in resolution eligibility unless the dossier is intentionally revised.


9. Current Card and result-state terminology remain separate
------------------------------------------------------------

This pass does not collapse Starting Role, Current Card, Current Allegiance, historical Raider, conversion status, protection, bond, mark, or caught state. The most important implementation rule is still that a convenience label must not silently substitute one axis for another. The package should continue to say STARTING ROLE when it means immutable action identity, CURRENT CARD when it means the movable card at the seat, and RAIDER when it means the historical thief.

Result screens should distinguish CAUGHT from BOND-REVEALED. A Guardian-protected player is not cleared. A converted Hunter remains historically the starting Hunter. A Bandit Current Card moved onto a Business starter does not make that player the historical Raider. These distinctions are especially important to bot explanation and replay because an apparently contradictory sequence may be perfectly legal when timestamps and state axes are preserved.


10. Bot parity and client-side validation
-----------------------------------------

The stronger target filters apply equally to bots. A bot must never choose itself for Paw Point, Guardian protection, Hunter mark, Business inspection, Dooker Glimpse/Trip, Trouble swap, or Snuggler bond. Existing bot target ranking already starts from other participants, but the general client safeguards give another layer of protection if a future role brain returns a malformed target.

Bot parity does not mean bots must deliberately make the same mistakes humans make. It means they receive no information advantage and obey the same legal-action boundaries. A bot can track its authorized facts perfectly, but it cannot read another player's private prompt, hidden card, raw die, or unrevealed conversion merely because code running in the same application could theoretically access it. It can use public chat, its own observations, its own legal private facts, and deductions from those sources.

Every bot action should be explainable after the round in the vocabulary of the dossier: own wake observation, Treat State, co-waker relation, legal scan/inspection result, public claim contradiction, known starting relationship, conversion fact known to that bot, or strategic inference. A hidden server truth that never entered the bot's authorized view is not an acceptable reason.


11. Asset continuity requirements
---------------------------------

Ferret Frenzy's visual/audio identity is gameplay-adjacent content and must travel with the package. The twenty-four role-card images are not interchangeable placeholders: their counts and role/pattern mappings matter to presentation. The pre-crime bookshelf image and post-crime Minnow Treats scene implement the central object-state transition. The Ferret Frenzy logo appears in lobby/PWA/sleep presentation. The packaged audio supports lobby music, dice, night/ferret cues, and Morning Business/Paw Point context.

A dossier-focused code pass must not delete these assets simply because no asset itself changed. The same applies to the bot JSON response libraries. Each role keeps a distinct 250-response file, for 2,000 packaged responses total. A pass that preserves bot JavaScript but drops the response JSON is functionally incomplete even if static tests that do not fetch the JSON happen to pass.

Revision 3.2's integrity test asserts these high-value continuity points. The separate preservation manifest records the complete baseline file set so future passes can compare at release time.


12. Documentation continuity and authority
------------------------------------------

Revision 3.2 is the current dossier authority for this package, but the original Revision 3.0 dossier remains in `docs/` unchanged as a historical baseline. The Revision 3.1 Pass 3 audit is also retained. Keeping these documents prevents a newer concise audit from erasing the rationale behind earlier implementation choices.

The new Revision 3.2 dossier is additive: the eight role deep dives remain over their prior word-count minimums, the twenty-eight interaction section remains intact, and the Revision 3.1 system-contract section remains intact. Part IX adds packaging and legality contracts; it does not replace the earlier analysis. If a later revision changes gameplay rather than merely clarifying implementation, it should identify the exact prior rule it supersedes.

Help text is allowed to be shorter than the dossier but must not contradict it. Lobby How to Play, HUD Quick How to Play, role cards, bot doctrine, JSON metadata, tests, and package README should point to the same current semantics. Stale version labels are maintenance defects because they can mislead audit tooling even when runtime behavior is correct.


13. Backend boundary remains unchanged
--------------------------------------

Revision 3.2 does not create, replace, or modify a Google Apps Script backend file. The package continues to reference the tested endpoint through the existing configuration. Frontend validation added in this pass is not represented as authoritative security or state resolution. It is a user-experience and regression-hardening layer.

Any rule whose correctness depends on hidden information must ultimately be validated by the server: legal role target, vote lock, Bandit Raider selection, d6/d12 randomness, conversion, card movement, Treat State, Guardian protection, catch set, Hunt Mark, Bond-Reveal, primary winner, private fact filtering, and reconnect authorization. If a future backend audit is requested, it must be performed against the actual backend source or endpoint behavior rather than inferred solely from frontend code.


14. Revision 3.2 deterministic regression matrix
------------------------------------------------

The minimum new fixtures are: self-vote submitted to a helper is discarded; self-vote never appears in a simultaneous lock set; valid votes on another player still count; all valid votes aimed at a protected player produce an empty Paw Point catch set; a protected top leader is removed and the next positive eligible total becomes decisive; two eligible players tied at the highest positive total are both caught; malformed role prompt omitting `excludeSelf` still does not show self for another-player-only base actions; two-target UI cannot create duplicate targets; and all previously packaged high-value assets remain present.

These tests supplement rather than replace the existing bot, simulation, frontend-dossier, solo-bot, chat/vote/privacy, Revision 3 gameplay, and Revision 3 social-deduction tests. A release is accepted only if the entire accumulated suite passes after the changes.

For release review, record five layers when a gameplay fixture is involved: authoritative hidden state, authorized private state for each player, public discussion state, bot-visible state, and Results/replay state. A fix that reaches the correct winner by leaking hidden information is not correct. A fix that preserves privacy but removes a required game asset is also not correct. Revision 3.2 treats both gameplay integrity and package integrity as release requirements.


15. Release acceptance checklist
--------------------------------

A Revision 3.2 package is acceptable only when all of the following are true. The full prior playable package is present as the starting point. No user asset was intentionally dropped. All twenty-four card images remain. All packaged audio remains. Lobby and frenzy entry points remain. PWA files remain. Eight role bot JavaScript entry files and eight 250-response JSON libraries remain. Existing docs remain unless an explicit replacement reason is recorded. The new dossier and audit are included. No backend source was generated or altered. The full test suite passes.

Gameplay acceptance additionally requires: self-votes are blocked in the visible UI and reusable client helper; role self-targets are blocked for all current other-player-only powers; two-target actions remain distinct; protected-target votes remain recordable but excluded from capture; zero positive eligible votes means zero Paw Point catches; eligible positive ties catch all leaders; Morning Business claims stay unverified; Starting Role and Current Card remain separate; historical Raider remains player-bound; and bots remain within human-equivalent information boundaries.

The release ZIP should be compared with the preservation baseline before delivery. If it is dramatically smaller, packaging stops until the difference is explained. The default response to uncertainty is to keep the content, not to prune it. This is now an explicit Ferret Frenzy release rule for dossier passes.

Minimum requested interaction discussion: 20,000 words

The Revision 3.1 Part IV and Revision 3.2 Part IX counts are additional implementation/release-reconciliation material and are not used to satisfy the eight per-role minimums or the interaction minimum. The document therefore continues to satisfy the requested minimums without relying on duplicated mentions in core rules, source analysis, or appendices.
